E\geq E_{-}(\ell)
.

•

Subcase 2a:
E(x,p)<E_{-}(\ell)
. The geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)\leq E_{-}(\ell)
in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
are bounded, so
(x,p)\in\mathcal{B}
. In contrast, a geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)<E_{-}(\ell)
contained in
\{r<r_{-}(\ell)\}
crosses
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. So,
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}
if
E(x,p)\leq E_{-}(\ell)
in
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell)\}
.

•

Subcase 2b:
E(x,p)\geq E_{-}(\ell)
. The geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with particle energy
E(x,p)\in(E_{-}(\ell),1)
cross
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. Thus, if
E(x,p)\in[E_{-}(\ell),1]
, then
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}
. On the other hand, geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)\geq 1
and
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, either cross the future event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, or escape to infinity towards the future. So, in this last case
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}
.

We note that for orbits
\gamma_{x,p}
with particle energy
E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell)
contained in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
, we have
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}\,\cap\,\mathcal{B}
, since
(x,p)
can be obtained as limit points of
(x,p)
with
E<E_{-}(\ell)
, and limit points of
(x,p)
with
E>E_{-}(\ell)
.

Case 3:
\ell(x,p)\in[4M,\infty)
. In this case, the radial potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has also critical points at
r_{\pm}(\ell)
, but
E_{-}(\ell)=V_{\ell}(r_{-}(l))\geq 1
instead. We proceed to study two subcases: orbits
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E<1
or
E\geq 1
.

•

Subcase 3a:
E(x,p)<1
.
Geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)<1
contained in
\{r>r_{-}(\ell)\}
are bounded. So
(x,p)\in\mathcal{B}
when
E(x,p)\leq 1
in
r>r_{-}(\ell)
. In contrast, the orbits
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)<1
contained in
\{r<r_{-}(\ell)\}
cross
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. So,
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}
when
E(x,p)\leq 1
in
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell)\}
.

•

Subcase 3b:
E(x,p)\geq 1
. On the other hand, geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with
E(x,p)\in(1,E_{-}(\ell))\,\cup\,(E_{-}(\ell),\infty)
and
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
, either cross the future event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, or escape to infinity towards the future. So
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}
when
E(x,p)\in[1,\infty)
and
\ell\in[4M,\infty)
. Moreover, the geodesics in the union
\cup_{\ell\in(4M,\infty)}\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
of trapped spheres is also contained in
\mathcal{B}
by definition.

We note that for orbits
\gamma_{x,p}
with particle energy
E(x,p)=1
contained in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
, we have
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}\,\cap\,\mathcal{B}
, since
(x,p)
can be obtained as limit points of
(x,p)
with
E<1
, and limit points of
(x,p)
with
E>1
. We also note that the union
\cup_{\ell\in(4M,\infty)}\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
of trapped spheres is also contained in the intersection
\mathcal{D}\,\cap\,\mathcal{B}
.

Putting together the previous three cases, we obtain that if
(x,p)
belongs to the RHS of (
26
)–(
27
), then
(x,p)
belongs to either
\mathcal{D}
or
\mathcal{B}
, respectively. A posteriori, it is straightforward to show the opposite, namely, that if
(x,p)
belongs to either
\mathcal{D}
or
\mathcal{B}
, then
(x,p)
belongs to the RHS of (
26
) or (
27
), respectively. The invariance of the sets
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{B}
is a direct consequence of the characterisations (
26
)–(
27
). The identity (
28
) also follows from the previous analysis.
∎

Remark 4.3.1
.

In the characterisation of
\mathcal{B}
, the union
\cup_{\ell\in(4M,\infty)}\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
does not play any role for the purpose of studying massive Vlasov fields on
\mathcal{B}
. We note that the closure of this set is given by
\cup_{\ell\in[4M,\infty)}\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, which has measure zero on the mass-shell.

See
[
Cha83
, Section 19]
for more information on the qualitative description of the timelike geodesic flow in Schwarzschild.

4.3.2.
Dispersive properties of the domain
\mathcal{D}

In this subsection, we show some fundamental properties of the dispersive region
\mathcal{D}
. We first show that
\mathcal{D}
is the largest invariant open subset of
\mathcal{P}
, where we can expect to prove decay estimates for the massive Vlasov equation on Schwarzschild.

Proposition 4.3.2
.

The set
\mathring{\mathcal{D}}
is the largest invariant open subset of
\mathcal{P}
, where for almost every
(x,p)
the geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
either crosses
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, or escapes to infinity towards the future.

Proof.

By contradiction, we suppose that there exists a non-trivial invariant open subset
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
containing
\mathcal{D}
. By Proposition
4.3.1
, the mass-shell is decomposed as
\mathring{\mathcal{D}}\sqcup(\mathcal{D}\cap\mathcal{B})\sqcup\mathring{B}
. Let
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{D}
.

If
(x,p)\in\mathring{B}
, then there is a neighbourhood
A\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap\mathring{B}
of
(x,p)
, since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is open. We can assume that
A
is invariant, since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and
\mathring{B}
are also invariant. We have obtained then an open set
A
such that
A\subset\mathring{B}\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. In particular, the orbits in
A
are bounded and do not cross
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. Finally, we obtain the desired contradiction since
A
is open.

On the other hand, if
(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}\cap\mathcal{B}
, then there is a neighbourhood
A\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
of
(x,p)
, since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is open. Furthermore, there exists
(x^{\prime},p^{\prime})\in A\,\cap\,\mathring{B}
since
\mathcal{D}\,\cap\,\mathcal{B}
has codimension one. Then, there exists a neighbourhood
B\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap\mathring{\mathcal{B}}
of
(x^{\prime},p^{\prime})
. We can assume that
B
is invariant by the invariance of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap\mathring{\mathcal{B}}
. The rest of the proof follows as in the case where
(x,p)\in\mathring{B}
, but using
(x^{\prime},p^{\prime})
instead.
∎

We show now that massive Vlasov fields do not decay in general when supported on any invariant open set
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
larger than
\mathcal{D}.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

For every non-trivial invariant open set
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}
containing the domain
\mathring{\mathcal{D}}
, there exists non-trivial compactly supported stationary solutions
f
of the massive Vlasov equation such that
\supp(f)\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
4.3.2
, there exists an invariant open subset
A\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap\mathring{B}
. One can then easily show that there exists a stationary Vlasov field of the form
f(x,p)=\Phi(E(x,p),\ell(x,p))\chi(x,p)
with
\supp(f)\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. Here,
\Phi
is a regular non-negative function, and
\chi
a suitably chosen cut-off function.
∎

By straightforward arguments Proposition
4.3.2
and Proposition
4.3.3
can be suitably modified to make similar statements for the set
\mathring{\Sigma}\subset\pi^{-1}(\underline{C}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\cup\,C_{\mathrm{out}})
over the initial bifurcate hypersurface, and the class of initial data for the massive Vlasov equation on
\pi^{-1}(\underline{C}_{\mathrm{in}}\,\cup\,C_{\mathrm{out}})
. We will not pursue this here.

4.3.3.
Characterisation of the trapped set
\Gamma

Let us characterise the trapped set
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{D}
of Schwarzschild, in terms of the particle energy
E(x,p)
, and the total angular momentum
\ell(x,p)
.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

The trapped set
\Gamma
is characterised as

(30)

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}:\ell(x,p)\in[2\sqrt{3}M,4M),\quad E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell),\quad r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\,\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}:\ell(x,p)\in[4M,\infty),\quad E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell),\quad r=r_{-}(\ell)\Big\}.

Moreover, the trapped set
\Gamma
has measure zero on the mass-shell.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
4.3.1
, there exists a non-trivial trapped set for every
\ell\geq 2\sqrt{3}M
. For
\ell\in[2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, the trapped set is composed by the points
(x,p)
in the homoclinic orbits associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
. In other words, we consider the points
(x,p)
with
E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell)
in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
. This set includes the corresponding spheres of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
. For
\ell\in[4M,\infty)
, the trapped set is composed only by the points
(x,p)
in the spheres
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
. Finally, we observe that the subsets of
\Gamma
with
\ell\in[2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and
\ell\in[4M,\infty)
, have both codimension one. In particular,
\Gamma
has measure zero on the mass-shell.
∎

Let us define the subset
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}\subset\Sigma
given by

\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}:=\Big\{(x,p)\in\Sigma:\text{$\gamma_{x,p}$ is future-trapped}\Big\}.

Let us show now that
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}
has measure zero with respect to the induced volume form on
\Sigma
.

Proposition 4.3.5
.

The set
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}
has measure zero on
\pi^{-1}(\underline{C}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C_{\mathrm{out}})
. In particular, for almost every
(x,p)\in\Sigma
, the future of
(x,p)
along
\gamma_{x,p}
either crosses
\mathcal{H}^{+}
or is unbounded.

Proof.

Let
\Sigma_{\mathrm{pt}}
be the set of points
(x,p)
in
\Sigma
such that
\gamma_{x,p}
is past-trapped. Note that the union
\Sigma_{\mathrm{pt}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}
is given by

\Sigma_{\mathrm{pt}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}=\pi^{-1}(\underline{C}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C_{\mathrm{out}})\cap\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)\in[2\sqrt{3}M,\infty),\quad E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell)\Big\},

where
E_{-}(\ell)
defines the energy level of the orbits in the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
. Finally, we observe that
\Sigma_{\mathrm{pt}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathrm{ft}}
has codimension one, so it has measure zero. In particular, the set
\Sigma_{\mathrm{pt}}
has also measure zero.
∎

In a similar fashion, one can easily show that for almost every
(x,p)\in\Sigma_{1}
(or in
\Sigma_{0}
), the future of
(x,p)
along
\gamma_{x,p}
either crosses
\mathcal{H}^{+}
or escapes to infinity.

4.4.
Dispersive properties in the near-horizon and the far-away regions

In this subsection, we study dispersive properties of the geodesic flow in the near-horizon and the far-away regions. These properties are obtained by studying the geodesic equations for the null coordinates. By the mass-shell relation, the null momentum coordinates satisfy

(31)

\dfrac{4r^{2}\Omega^{2}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}p^{u}p^{v}=1.

Motivated by this identity, we will show suitable expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow towards the submanifolds of the mass-shell
\{\Omega^{2}p^{u}=0\}
and
\{p^{v}=0\}
. We note that the submanifolds
\{\Omega^{2}p^{u}=0\}
and
\{p^{v}=0\}
intersect at the tip of the light cones
\mathcal{P}_{x}
for every point in spacetime.

4.4.1.
In the far-away region

Let us consider the normalised momentum coordinates
\frac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}
and
\Omega^{2}p^{v}
. The product of these normalised coordinates is constant by (
31
). In the following, it will be convenient to parametrise timelike geodesics by the retarded time coordinate
u
. We define the derivative along the geodesic flow with respect to
u
as
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}:=\frac{1}{p^{u}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Along any timelike geodesic on the exterior of Schwarzschild with angular momentum
\ell
, we have

(32)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\Big(\dfrac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}\Big),

(33)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}(\Omega^{2}p^{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)(\Omega^{2}p^{v}).

By integrating the geodesic equations (
32
)–(
33
) for the normalised momentum coordinates, we obtain the following constants of motion along the geodesic flow:

\displaystyle\dfrac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(0)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(s)\exp\Big(\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\Big),

\displaystyle\Omega^{2}p^{v}(0)

\displaystyle=\Omega^{2}p^{v}(s)\exp\Big(-\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\Big).

We remark that the term
\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})
is positive when
r
is sufficiently large. As a result, we obtain expansion and contraction for the geodesic flow towards the submanifolds of the mass-shell
\{\Omega^{2}p^{u}=0\}
and
\{p^{v}=0\}
, respectively.

Remark 4.4.1
.

The normalised momentum coordinate
\frac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}
satisfies the relation
\frac{r^{2}p^{u}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}=g(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\Omega^{2}(\ell^{2}+r^{2})}\partial_{v},p).
The vector field
-\frac{r^{2}}{2\Omega^{2}(\ell^{2}+r^{2})}\partial_{v}
is a modification of the vector field
r^{2}\partial_{v}
that is used in the well-known
r^{p}
-weighted energy method
introduced by Dafermos–Rodnianski for the study of wave equations on black hole spacetimes
[
DR10
]
. On the other hand, the normalised coordinate
\Omega^{2}p^{v}
satisfies the relation
\Omega^{2}p^{v}=g(-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{u},p)
.

4.4.2.
In the near-horizon region

Let us consider the normalised momentum coordinates
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
and
\frac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}
. The product of these normalised coordinates is constant by (
31
). In the following, it will be convenient to parametrise timelike geodesics by the advanced time coordinate
v
. We define the derivative along the geodesic flow with respect to
v
as
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}v}:=\frac{1}{p^{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
.

Proposition 4.4.2
.

Along any timelike geodesic on the exterior of Schwarzschild with angular momentum
\ell
, we have

(34)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}v}(\Omega^{2}p^{u})

\displaystyle=\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)(\Omega^{2}p^{u}),

(35)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}v}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\Big(\dfrac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}\Big).

By integrating the geodesic equations (
34
)–(
35
) for the normalised momentum coordinates, we obtain the following constants of motion along the geodesic flow:

\displaystyle\dfrac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(0)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(s)\exp\Big(\int_{v(0)}^{v(s)}\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\Big),

\displaystyle\Omega^{2}p^{u}(0)

\displaystyle=\Omega^{2}p^{u}(s)\exp\Big(-\int_{v(0)}^{v(s)}\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\Big).

We remark that the term
\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})|_{r=2M}
is equal to
\frac{1}{2M}
for every
\ell\geq 0
. The value
\frac{1}{2M}
corresponds to the surface gravity of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. More generally, the term
\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})
is positive when
r\sim 2M
. As a result, we obtain expansion and contraction for the geodesic flow towards the submanifolds of the mass-shell
\{p^{v}=0\}
and
\{\Omega^{2}p^{u}=0\}
, respectively.

Remark 4.4.2
.

We note that the normalised momentum coordinate
\frac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}
satisfies the relation
\frac{r^{2}p^{v}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}=g(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\Omega^{2}(\ell^{2}+r^{2})}\partial_{u},p).
The vector field
-\frac{r^{2}}{2\Omega^{2}(\ell^{2}+r^{2})}\partial_{u}
is a modification of the vector field
\Omega^{-2}\partial_{v}
that is used to exploit the well-known
red-shift effect
in the study of wave equations on black hole spacetimes. See the lecture notes
[
DR13
, Chapter 3]
for more information. On the other hand, the normalised coordinate
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
satisfies the relation
\Omega^{2}p^{u}=g(-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{v},p)
.

4.5.
Estimates for the momentum coordinates along timelike geodesics

In this subsection, we prove some elementary a priori estimates for the momentum coordinates along geodesics in the exterior of Schwarzschild. We first address the case of geodesics that are not contained in the near-horizon region.

In the rest of the paper, the following terminology will be useful. We say that a geodesic
\gamma
is
outgoing
or
ingoing at a point

x\in\mathcal{E}
, if
p^{r}>0
or
p^{r}<0
, respectively. We also say that a geodesic
\gamma
is
outgoing
or
ingoing in a region

\mathcal{R}
, if
\gamma
is outgoing or ingoing for every
x\in\mathcal{R}
, respectively. We sometimes say that a geodesic is simply outgoing or ingoing whenever the meaning is clear from the context.

Lemma 4.5.1
.

Let
r_{0}>2M
,
L_{1}>0
, and
E_{1}>0
. For every geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r>r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L_{1}
and particle energy
E\leq E_{1}
, the momentum coordinates along
\gamma
satisfy

|p^{u}(s)|\lesssim 1,\qquad|p^{v}(s)|\lesssim 1,\qquad|p^{\theta}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{r^{2}(s)},\qquad|\sin\theta p^{\phi}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{r^{2}(s)}.

Proof.

By the definition of
E
, the null momentum coordinates are bounded by

|p^{u}(s)+p^{v}(s)|=\dfrac{E}{\Omega^{2}(r(s))}\leq\dfrac{E_{1}}{\Omega^{2}(r_{0})}.

We have used here the conservation of
E
along the geodesic flow. On the other hand, the spherical momentum coordinates satisfy

\sqrt{(p^{\theta})^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta(p^{\phi})^{2}}=\frac{\ell}{r^{2}(s)}\leq\frac{L_{1}}{r^{2}(s)}.

We have used here the conservation of
\ell
along the geodesic flow.
∎

We obtain next explicit formulae for the null momentum coordinates in terms of the radial coordinate, and the conserved quantities of the corresponding timelike geodesic. This formulae follow from the mass-shell relation.

Lemma 4.5.2
.

Let
\gamma
be a geodesic with angular momentum
\ell
and particle energy
E
. Then, the null momentum coordinates
p^{u}
,
p^{v}
along
\gamma
are equal to

(36)

\dfrac{E}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(1\pm\dfrac{1}{E}\sqrt{\dfrac{(E^{2}-1)r^{3}+2Mr^{2}-\ell^{2}r+2M\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}}\Big),

where the sign is chosen depending on whether the geodesic is ingoing or outgoing at
\gamma(s)
.

Proof.

By the mass-shell relation and the definition of
E
, we have

\dfrac{E}{\Omega^{2}}=\dfrac{1}{4\Omega^{2}p^{v}}\Big(1+\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)+p^{v}.

We rewrite this identity as the quadratic equation

(p^{v})^{2}-\dfrac{E}{\Omega^{2}}p^{v}+\dfrac{1}{4\Omega^{2}}\Big(1+\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)=0.

We note that the same quadratic equation is satisfied by
p^{u}
. Thus, the null momentum coordinates are equal to

\dfrac{E}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(1\pm\sqrt{1-\frac{V_{\ell}(r)}{E^{2}}}\Big),

where the sign is chosen depending on whether the geodesic is ingoing or outgoing at
x\in\mathcal{E}
.
∎

4.5.1.
The momentum coordinates in the far-away region

We will now perform estimates for the momentum coordinates along outgoing geodesics in the far-away region of spacetime.

Proposition 4.5.3
.

Let
E_{1}>E_{0}>1
and
L_{1}>0
. There exists
R>2M
such that every geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r>R\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L_{1}
and particle energy
E\in[1,E_{1}]
is either outgoing or ingoing. Moreover, for every outgoing geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r>R\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L_{1}
and particle energy
E\in[1,E_{1}]
, the null momentum coordinates along
\gamma
satisfy

(37)

\Big|p^{u}(s)-\dfrac{E-\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(s)},\qquad\Big|p^{v}(s)-\dfrac{E+\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(s)},

Finally, for every outgoing geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r>R\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L_{1}
and particle energy
E\in[E_{0},E_{1}]
, the null momentum coordinates along
\gamma
satisfy

\Big|p^{u}(s)-\dfrac{E-\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r(s)},\qquad\Big|p^{v}(s)-\dfrac{E+\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r(s)}.

Proof.

By the mass-shell relation, the radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
along a geodesic
\gamma
is bounded below by

(38)

(p^{r})^{2}=E^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)=\dfrac{r^{3}(E^{2}-1)+2Mr^{2}-\ell^{2}r+2M\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}\geq\dfrac{2Mr^{2}-\ell^{2}r+2M\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}\gtrsim\dfrac{1}{r},

by choosing
R>2M
sufficiently large. We are using here that
\gamma
is contained in
\{r\geq R\}
. In particular, every geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r\geq R\}
is either ingoing or outgoing.

We now restrict our attention to outgoing geodesics. In this case, the null momentum coordinates of a geodesic
\gamma
can be written as

p^{u}=\dfrac{E}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{V_{\ell}(r)}{E^{2}}}\Big),\qquad\quad p^{v}=\dfrac{E}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(1+\sqrt{1-\frac{V_{\ell}(r)}{E^{2}}}\Big),

by Lemma
4.5.2
. We write the difference between the momentum coordinate
p^{v}
and its limit value at infinity as

(39)

p^{v}(s)-\dfrac{E+\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2}=\dfrac{EM}{\Omega^{2}r}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Big(\sqrt{\dfrac{E^{2}}{\Omega^{4}}-\dfrac{V_{\ell}(r)}{\Omega^{4}}}-\sqrt{E^{2}-1}\Big).

We first address the case when
E\geq 1
. We bound the second term in (
39
) by

\displaystyle\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{E^{2}}{\Omega^{4}}-\dfrac{V_{\ell}(r)}{\Omega^{4}}}-\sqrt{E^{2}-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\dfrac{E^{2}}{\Omega^{4}}(1-\Omega^{4})+\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{4}}(\Omega^{4}-V_{\ell}(r))\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\Big|\dfrac{2M\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}+4M^{2}E^{2}}{r}+2M(2E^{2}-1)\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}},

where we used the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root in the first estimate. The estimate for
p^{v}
then follows, since the first term in (
39
) decays faster. A similar argument proves the corresponding estimate for
p^{u}
.

On the other hand, if
E\geq E_{0}>1
, then the second term in (
39
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{E^{2}}{\Omega^{4}}-\dfrac{V_{\ell}(r)}{\Omega^{4}}}-\sqrt{E^{2}-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\dfrac{E^{2}}{\Omega^{4}}(1-\Omega^{4})+\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{4}}(\Omega^{4}-V_{\ell}(r))\Big|

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{r\Omega^{4}}\Big|\dfrac{2M\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}+4M^{2}E^{2}}{r}+2M(2E^{2}-1)\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{r},

where we used in the first estimate that the square root is Lipschitz strictly away of the origin. The estimate for
p^{v}
then follows, since the first term in (
39
) decays with the same rate. A similar argument proves the corresponding estimate for
p^{u}
.
∎

Remark 4.5.1
.

The decay rate
r^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the bounds (
37
) for the momentum coordinates is optimal. For geodesics with
E=1
, we have

p^{v}-\dfrac{1}{2}=\dfrac{M}{\Omega^{2}r}+\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\sqrt{1-V_{\ell}(r)}=\dfrac{M}{\Omega^{2}r}+\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{\Omega^{2}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

A similar identity holds for the difference
p^{u}\,-\,\frac{1}{2}
when
E=1
. This energy value (equal to one) corresponds to the rest mass of the particles in the system.

4.5.2.
The momentum coordinates in the near-horizon region

We estimate the null momentum coordinates along ingoing geodesics in a neighbourhood of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. In the following, we will estimate
Dp^{u}
instead of
p^{u}
, because
\Omega^{-2}\partial_{u}
extends regularly to a non-vanishing vector field on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Lemma 4.5.4
.

Let
E_{0}>0
and
L>0
. There exists
r_{0}>2M
such that every geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r<r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L
and particle energy
E\in[E_{0},\infty)
, is either outgoing or ingoing. Moreover, for every ingoing geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r<r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq L
and particle energy
E\in[E_{0},\infty)
, the null momentum coordinates along
\gamma
satisfy

|E-\Omega^{2}p^{u}(s)|\lesssim\Omega(s),\qquad\Big|p^{v}(s)-\dfrac{1}{4E}\Big(\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{4M^{2}}+1\Big)\Big|\lesssim\Omega(s).

Proof.

By the mass-shell relation, the radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
along a geodesic
\gamma
satisfies

(p^{r})^{2}=E^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)\geq E_{0}^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)>\frac{1}{2}E_{0}^{2},

by choosing
r_{0}-2M
sufficiently small. Thus, every such geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r<r_{0}\}
is either outgoing or ingoing.

We now restrict our attention to incoming geodesics. In this case, we note that
p^{r}(s)=\Omega^{2}p^{v}(s)-\Omega^{2}p^{u}(s)<0
. Thus, we can bound the term
\Omega^{2}p^{v}
by

(40)

\Omega^{2}p^{v}\leq(\Omega^{2}p^{u})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega^{2}p^{v})^{\frac{1}{2}}=\dfrac{1}{2}\Omega\Big(\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}+1\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\Omega

where we have used the mass-shell relation. As a result, we obtain the desired estimate for
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
by

(41)

|E-\Omega^{2}p^{u}(s)|=\Omega^{2}p^{v}(s)\leq C\Omega(s),

where
C>0
is a uniform constant. In particular, we obtain a uniform lower bound for
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
, since

\Omega^{2}p^{u}\geq E-C\Omega(r)\geq E_{0}-C\Omega(r)\geq\frac{1}{2}E_{0},

by choosing
r_{0}-2M
sufficiently small. Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|p^{v}-\dfrac{1}{4E}\Big(\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{4M^{2}}+1\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{4}\Big(\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{2}p^{u}r^{2}}-\dfrac{1}{4M^{2}E}\Big)+\dfrac{1}{4}\Big(\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{2}p^{u}}-\dfrac{1}{E}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{4\Omega^{2}p^{u}}\Big(\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}-\dfrac{1}{4M^{2}}\Big)+\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{16M^{2}}\Big(\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{2}p^{u}}-\dfrac{1}{E}\Big)+\dfrac{E-\Omega^{2}p^{u}}{4E\Omega^{2}p^{u}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\dfrac{\ell^{2}(2M-r)(2M+r)}{16M^{2}\Omega^{2}r^{2}p^{u}}+\dfrac{\ell^{2}(E-\Omega^{2}p^{u})}{16M^{2}E\Omega^{2}p^{u}}+\dfrac{E-\Omega^{2}p^{u}}{4E\Omega^{2}p^{u}}\Big|\lesssim\Omega,

where we used in the last inequality the estimate (
41
), and the lower bounds for
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
and
E
.
∎

5.
The trapped set and the stable manifolds

In this section, we study the geodesic flow in a neighbourhood of the trapped set
\Gamma
in the dispersive region
\mathcal{D}
of phase space. In this analysis, an important role is played by the stable manifolds associated to the spheres of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
for
\ell\geq 2\sqrt{3}M
. We will show suitable contraction and expansion properties for the geodesic flow, by using suitable defining functions of the stable manifolds. We will also estimate the corresponding rates of contraction and expansion. We finish this section by studying similar properties for the parabolic trapping at infinity.

5.1.
Unstable trapping

Let
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
. By Proposition
4.2.1
,
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
is the unique sphere containing geodesics with angular momentum
\ell
. One can easily show the normal hyperbolicity of the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
by using variants of the arguments in
[
WZ11
]
, because of the hyperbolicity of the fixed point
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
of the radial flow.
2
2

2

Specifically, the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
of trapped orbits is
eventually absolutely
r
-normally hyperbolic
for every
r
according to
[
HPS77
, Chapter 1, Definition 4]
.
The normal hyperbolicity of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
implies the existence of suitable stable and unstable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
in phase space
\mathcal{P}
. This property follows by the powerful stable manifold theorem for normally hyperbolic sets
[
HPS77
]
. We remark that the orbits in the stable and the unstable manifolds, are future-trapped and past-trapped, respectively.

For the geodesic flow in Schwarzschild, we can write the submanifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
explicitly, since they correspond to the energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
of the radial flow. For this purpose, we parametrise the radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
of future-trapped and past-trapped orbits with
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
and
E=E_{-}(\ell)
, by

(42)

p^{r,-}_{\ell}(r):=\mathrm{sgn}(r_{-}(\ell)-r)\sqrt{E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-V_{\ell}(r)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r):=\mathrm{sgn}(r-r_{-}(\ell))\sqrt{E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-V_{\ell}(r)},

respectively. We summarise this discussion with the following propositions.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

Let
\ell>4M
. The stable manifolds of the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
are analytic codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
, given by

\displaystyle W^{+}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,+}_{\ell}\Big\},

\displaystyle W^{-}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,-}_{\ell}\Big\}.

In particular, the intersection
W^{+}(\ell)\,\cap\,W^{-}(\ell)
is equal to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
.

We note that the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
are contained in the unbounded region
\{r>2M\}
. By the mass-shell relation, we have the constraint that
E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-V_{\ell}(r)\geq 0
which is satisfied for any
r>2M
.

Proposition 5.1.2
.

Let
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. The stable manifolds of the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
are analytic codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
, given by

\displaystyle W^{+}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\quad p^{r}=p^{r,+}_{\ell}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r\geq r_{-}(\ell),\quad p^{r}=p^{r,\pm}_{\ell}\Big\},

\displaystyle W^{-}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\quad p^{r}=p^{r,-}_{\ell}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r\geq r_{-}(\ell),\quad p^{r}=p^{r,\pm}_{\ell}\Big\}.

In particular, the intersection
W^{+}(\ell)\,\cap\,W^{-}(\ell)
is equal to the set

W_{\mathrm{hom}}(\ell):=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r\geq r_{-}(\ell),\quad p^{r}=p^{r,\pm}_{\ell}\Big\}.

If
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
are only contained in the domain
\{2M<r\leq a(\ell)\}
, where
a(\ell)
is the unique root of
E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-V_{\ell}(r)
on
(r_{-}(\ell),\infty)
. Recall that by the mass-shell relation, we have the constraint that
E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-V_{\ell}(r)\geq 0
.

Remark 5.1.1
.

(a)

For
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, the orbits in the stable manifolds with
r>r_{-}(\ell)
, are of
homoclinic type
. In other words, these orbits satisfy
\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}(r(s),p^{r}(s))=(r_{-}(\ell),0)
. For this reason, the intersection of the stable manifolds is given by
W_{\mathrm{hom}}(\ell)
. We note that
W_{\mathrm{hom}}(\ell)
is contained in the trapped set
\Gamma
.

(b)

We clarify here an abuse of terminology used in Proposition
5.1.2
. For
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
are
not submanifolds
of
{\mathcal{P}}
due to global considerations. The stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
self-intersect at the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
. This property holds because the set
W_{\mathrm{hom}}(\ell)
, where we find homoclinic orbits with angular momentum
\ell
, is contained in the stable and the unstable manifolds. Despite this global misbehaviour, the sets
W^{\pm}(\ell)
are indeed codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
in a
neighbourhood of the sphere

\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
.

5.1.1.
Lyapunov exponents of the fixed points
(r_{-}(\ell),0)

Let
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
. Let us study the infinitesimal rates of contraction and expansion of the radial flow on the spheres of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
.

It will be convenient to parametrise timelike geodesics by the time coordinate
t
. We define the derivative along the geodesic flow with respect to
t
as
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}:=\frac{1}{p^{t}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
. In these terms, we write the radial geodesic equation as

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}r}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{(p^{t})^{3}}\Big(\dfrac{\mathrm{d}p^{r}}{\mathrm{d}s}p^{t}-\dfrac{\mathrm{d}p^{t}}{\mathrm{d}s}p^{r}\Big)=-\dfrac{M\Omega^{4}}{E^{2}r^{4}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)+\dfrac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{M}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(\Omega^{4}-\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)^{2}\Big)\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)+\dfrac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)^{2}

(43)

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{M\Omega^{4}}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)+\dfrac{3M}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{3M}r+\dfrac{5\ell^{2}}{3}\Big)\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)^{2}.

The radial geodesic equation (
43
) defines the radial flow in the
(r,\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}t})
coordinates by

(44)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}t}=\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}},\qquad\quad\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)=-\dfrac{M\Omega^{4}}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)+\dfrac{3M}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{3M}r+\dfrac{5\ell^{2}}{3}\Big)\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)^{2}.

The sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
of trapped orbits corresponds to the fixed point
(r=r_{-}(\ell),\,p^{r}=0)
for
\ell\geq 2\sqrt{3}M
. The
linearisation of the radial flow (
44
) around the fixed point

(r_{-}(\ell),0)
is given by

(45)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(r-r_{-}(\ell))=\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}},\qquad\quad\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{t}}\Big)=\Phi_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell))(r-r_{-}(\ell)),

where the function
\Phi_{\ell}\colon[2M,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Phi_{\ell}(r):=~

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\Big(-\dfrac{M\Omega^{4}}{r^{4}V_{\ell}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\Big).

An elementary computation shows that

(46)

\lambda(\ell):=\Phi_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell))=\frac{M\Omega^{2}(r_{-}(\ell))}{r^{2}_{-}(\ell)(r_{-}^{2}(\ell)+\ell^{2})}(r_{+}(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell)),

by using that
r_{\pm}(\ell)
satisfies
r^{2}_{\pm}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r_{\pm}+3\ell^{2}=0
. As a result, we have
\Phi_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell))>0
when
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
, and
\Phi_{2\sqrt{3}M}(6M)=0
when
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. In particular, the fixed point
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
of the radial flow is hyperbolic when
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
, and the hyperbolicity
degenerates
at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
.

Definition 5.1.3
.

Let
\lambda\colon(2\sqrt{3}M,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be defined by

\lambda(\ell):=\Phi_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell)).

The
positive and negative Lyapunov exponents of the fixed point

(r_{-}(\ell),0)
, are
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
and
-\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
, respectively. From now on, we refer to
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
simply as the
Lyapunov exponent of

(r_{-}(\ell),0)
.

The Lyapunov exponent
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
determines the infinitesimal rate of expansion and contraction of the radial flow on the unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. In other words, the Lyapunov exponent
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
determines the rate of expansion and contraction of the differential of the radial flow on the stable and unstable subspaces (which are tangent to the stable and unstable manifolds), respectively.

We will now show that the Lyapunov exponent
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
is strictly increasing in
\ell
. We begin proving that
r_{-}(\ell)
is a monotone function.

Lemma 5.1.4
.

The derivative of the function
r_{-}\colon[2\sqrt{3}M,\infty)\to(3M,6M]
is

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}=-\frac{2M}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell).

In particular, we have
r_{-}(\ell_{2})\leq r_{-}(\ell_{1})
for all
l_{2}\geq l_{1}
.

Proof.

The monotonicity of
r_{-}(\ell)
follows directly by proving that
\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}\geq 0
. We compute the derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{M}\Big(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big)-\frac{6M}{\ell}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{M}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}-\Big(1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\Big)-\frac{6M}{\ell^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\ell}{2M}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big)^{2}=-\frac{2M}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell),

where we used the definition of
r_{-}(\ell)
in the last line. As a result, we obtain the desired monotonicity property of
r_{-}(\ell)
.
∎

We prove next the desired monotonicity property for the Lyapunov exponent
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
.

Proposition 5.1.5
.

The derivative of the function
\lambda\colon(2\sqrt{3}M,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
is

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\mathrm{d}\ell}=\frac{M^{2}}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{r_{-}(\ell)(r_{-}(\ell)-3M)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big)\Big(1+4\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big).

In particular, we have
\lambda(\ell_{2})\geq\lambda(\ell_{1})
for all
l_{2}\geq l_{1}
.

Proof.

The monotonicity of
\lambda(\ell)
follows directly by proving that
\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\mathrm{d}\ell}\geq 0
. We first rewrite the function
\lambda(\ell)
as

\lambda(\ell):=\frac{M\Omega^{2}(r_{-}(\ell))}{r^{2}_{-}(\ell)(r_{-}^{2}(\ell)+\ell^{2})}(r_{+}(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))=\frac{M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\frac{1}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-3Mr_{-}(\ell)}\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}},

where we used the definition of
r_{\pm}(\ell)
. We compute the derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\mathrm{d}\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2M^{2}}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{r_{-}^{2}-3Mr_{-}}\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}-\frac{M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\frac{2r_{-}-3M}{(r_{-}^{2}-3Mr_{-})^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{12M^{4}}{\ell^{5}}\frac{1}{r_{-}^{2}-3Mr_{-}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2M^{2}}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{r_{-}^{2}-3Mr_{-}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(\frac{18M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-1\Big)+\frac{2M^{3}}{\ell^{5}}\frac{2r_{-}-3M}{(r_{-}^{2}-3Mr_{-})^{2}}r_{-}^{2}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\frac{2M^{2}}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{r_{-}(r_{-}-3M)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(\frac{18M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-1+\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}+\frac{Mr_{-}}{\ell^{2}}\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{M^{2}}{\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{r_{-}(r_{-}-3M)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}}\Big(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big)\Big(1+4\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\Big),

where we used Lemma
5.1.4
in the second equality, and the definition of
r_{-}(\ell)
in the last two lines. As a result, we obtain the desired monotonicity property of
\lambda(\ell).

∎

5.1.2.
Expansion and contraction of the radial flow with
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)

Let us set the function
H_{\ell}\colon[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

H_{\ell}(E):=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)},

with
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. Note that
H_{\ell}(E)
is conserved along the geodesic flow when the angular momentum is
\ell
. By Proposition
5.1.1
, the union
W^{+}(\ell)\,\cup\,W^{-}(\ell)
of the stable manifolds associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, is characterised as
\{H_{\ell}(E)=0\}
.

It will be convenient to parametrise timelike geodesics by the time coordinate
\bar{t}=s(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
in the domain
\{E<1\}
. We define the derivative along the geodesic flow with respect to
\bar{t}
as
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{t}}=(1-E^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
.

Proposition 5.1.6
.

Let us consider the radial flow with
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. The quantity
H_{\ell}(E)
satisfies

(47)

H_{\ell}(E)=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}+\Big(1+\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{2}\Big),

where

a(\ell)=\frac{2M^{2}r_{-}(\ell)^{3}}{\ell^{2}(4M-r_{-}(\ell))(r_{-}(\ell)-3M)}.

Set the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\,\cap\,\{E<1\}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}(x,p):=\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\frac{p^{r}}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\pm\Big(-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big).

Then, the derivative of
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
along the geodesic flow is

(48)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{t}}=\mp\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{+}(\ell))}{2(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}.

Proof.

Rearranging the mass-shell relation (
23
), we have

(49)

\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{E^{2}-1}-1=\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{r-2M}{r^{3}}=\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}.

Evaluating this identity on
(r=r_{-}(\ell),\,p^{r}=0)
, we obtain

(50)

\frac{\ell^{2}}{E_{-}^{2}(\ell)-1}=-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}\cdot\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}=\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{4M-r_{-}},

where we used
r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r_{-}+3\ell^{2}=0
in the last equality. Putting together the identities (
49
) and (
50
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}-\frac{2M}{E_{-}^{2}(\ell)-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\cdot\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{E^{2}-1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\Big(r^{3}+\frac{2Mr_{-}^{3}}{\ell^{2}(4M-r_{-})}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)\Big).

To obtain (
47
), it is enough to show that
a(\ell)
satisfies

(51)

r^{3}+\frac{2Mr_{-}^{3}}{\ell^{2}(4M-r_{-})}r^{2}-\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{4M-r_{-}}r+\frac{2Mr_{-}^{3}}{4M-r_{-}}=(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-})^{2}.

Using the equation
r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r_{-}+3\ell^{2}=0
, we first write

\displaystyle(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-})^{2}

\displaystyle=(r+a(\ell))\Big(r^{2}-2rr_{-}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}(r_{-}-3M)\Big)

(52)

\displaystyle=r^{3}+(a(\ell)-2r_{-})r^{2}+\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}(r_{-}-3M)-2a(\ell)r_{-}\Big)r+\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}(r_{-}-3M)a(\ell).

The identity (
51
) follows if and only if the corresponding coefficients are equal, in other words, if

(53)

a(\ell)=2r_{-}+\frac{2Mr_{-}^{3}}{\ell^{2}(4M-r_{-})},\qquad\quad a(\ell)=\frac{1}{2r_{-}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}(r_{-}-3M)+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{4M-r_{-}}\Big).

The identities (
53
) are proved by an elementary computation using
r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r_{-}+3\ell^{2}=0
.

Before proceeding, we note that the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
are well-defined. On the one hand,
r_{-}+a(\ell)>0
when
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. Furthermore, we have

r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}=(r-\frac{\ell^{2}}{4M})^{2}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{16M^{2}}(16M^{2}-\ell^{2})\geq 0

when
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. Thus, the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
are well-defined on
{\mathcal{P}}\,\cap\,\{E<1\}
.

We show next the relation (
48
). We first compute the derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}p^{r}}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)r^{\frac{3}{2}}p^{r}+\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}p^{r})

\displaystyle=\frac{(p^{r})^{2}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{\ell^{2}}{4M}-r)}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\frac{3}{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(p^{r})^{2}-\frac{M}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(p^{r})^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)-\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{M}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)

(54)

\displaystyle=\frac{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}(E^{2}-1),

where we used the mass-shell relation in the last equality. Secondly, we compute the derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{(-r-a(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-})}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)(-r-a(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big((-r-a(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{p^{r}(-a(\ell)-r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{2(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(2\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{4M}-r\Big)(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-})+(3r+2a(\ell)-r_{-})\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)\Big).

An explicit computation using
r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r_{-}+3\ell^{2}=0
and the definition of
a(\ell)
, shows that

2\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{4M}-r\Big)(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-})+(3r+2a(\ell)-r_{-})\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)=r^{3}-r^{2}r_{+}.

Hence, we have

(55)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-})}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)=\frac{p^{r}r^{2}(r-r_{+})}{2(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Putting together (
54
) and (
55
), we obtain (
48
).
∎

We observe that the term in the RHS of (
48
) satisfies

\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{+}(\ell))}{2(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\Big|_{r=r_{-}(\ell)}<0.

This observation shows the local expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow near the unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. By the identity (
48
), the Lyapunov exponent of
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
can also be written as

\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)=\frac{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2E_{-}(\ell)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))(r_{-}(\ell)-2M)}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-}(-a(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}_{-}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2})}.

Remark 5.1.2
.

The zero sets of the functions
\varphi_{+}^{\ell}
and
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
define
only subsets
of the unstable and stable manifolds associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, respectively. In other words, for all
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, we have

(56)

W^{\pm}_{\mathrm{in/out}}(\ell):=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad\varphi_{\mp}^{\ell}(x,p)=0\Big\}\subset W^{\pm}(\ell).

We note that the geodesics in the subsets
W^{\pm}_{\mathrm{in/out}}(\ell)
of the stable manifolds are always either ingoing or outgoing. In particular, the sets
W^{\pm}_{\mathrm{in/out}}(\ell)
only contain the ingoing or the outgoing parts of the homoclinic orbits in the energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
.

Next, we will show the exponential rate of contraction and expansion of the geodesic flow on the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
in a neighbourhood of
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
. We show that the rate of contraction and expansion can be taken arbitrarily close to the Lyapunov exponent in a small neighbourhood of
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
.

Proposition 5.1.7
.

Let
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and
\delta\in(0,\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell))
. There exists
\epsilon>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\lesssim\epsilon\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{-}(\ell)
, we have

(57)

|r(t(s))-r_{-}(\ell)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s))},\qquad|p^{r}(t(s))|\leq\frac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s))}

for all
s\in[0,a]
. Moreover, for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[-a,0]\to\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(\ell)
, we have

(58)

|r(t(-s))-r_{-}(\ell)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)|t(-s)|)},\qquad|p^{r}(t(-s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)|t(-s)|)},

for all
s\in[-a,0]
.

Proof.

We suppose first that
(x,p)\in W^{-}(\ell)
. By the invariance of the stable manifold
W^{-}(\ell)
, we have that
(x(s),p(s))\in W^{-}(\ell)
for all
s\geq 0
. Assuming that
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small (depending on
\ell
), we have that
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}\cap W^{-}(\ell)
is a subset of
\{\varphi_{+}^{\ell}=0\}
, so

\frac{p^{r}}{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}=-\Big(-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big).

As a result, the defining function
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
satisfies the relations

(59)

\varphi_{-}^{\ell}(x,p)=2\frac{\omega_{\ell}(r)p^{r}}{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}=-2\omega_{\ell}(r)\Big(-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big),

where
\omega_{\ell}(r):=r^{\frac{3}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is a positive radial weight.

Next, we integrate the derivative (
48
) along the geodesic flow by

\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)=\varphi_{-}^{\ell}(x,p)\exp\Big(-\frac{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E_{-}(\ell)}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where here
(x(0)=x,\,p(0)=p)
. In the following, we set
\epsilon>0
small enough so that

\Big|\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\frac{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E_{-}(\ell)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\Big|\leq\delta,

for all
(x,p)\in\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}
. As a result, we obtain the upper bound

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{(1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E_{-}(\ell)}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\mathrm{d}t\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s)\Big).

Finally, we use the relations (
59
) to show that

|r(t(s))-r_{-}(\ell)|+|p^{r}(t(s))|\lesssim\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s)\Big),

where we have used suitable lower bounds for the factors in (
59
). The case of a geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(\ell)
follows similarly.
∎

5.1.3.
Expansion and contraction of the radial flow with
\ell=4M

Let us consider the conserved quantity along the geodesic flow given by

E^{2}-1.

By Proposition
5.1.1
, the union
W^{+}(4M)\,\cup\,W^{-}(4M)
of the stable manifolds associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(4M)
, is characterised as
\{E^{2}-1=0\}
.

Proposition 5.1.8
.

Let us consider the radial flow with
\ell=4M
. The quantity
E^{2}-1
satisfies

(60)

E^{2}-1=(p^{r})^{2}-\dfrac{2M}{r}\Big(1-\frac{4M}{r}\Big)^{2}.

Set the functions
\psi_{\pm}^{4M}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\psi_{\pm}^{4M}(x,p):=p^{r}\pm\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{4M}{r}\Big).

Then, the derivative of
\psi_{\pm}^{4M}
along the geodesic flow is

(61)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\psi_{\pm}^{4M}}{\mathrm{d}s}=\mp\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\psi_{\pm}^{4M}.

Proof.

The identity (
60
) follows directly by using the mass-shell relation (
23
) with
\ell=4M
. We show next (
61
). We first compute the derivative

(62)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{r-4M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big)=-\dfrac{p^{r}}{2r^{\frac{5}{2}}}(r-12M).

Moreover, the radial geodesic equation is

(63)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}p^{r}}{\mathrm{d}s}=-\dfrac{M}{r^{4}}\Big(r^{2}-16Mr+48M^{2}\Big)=-\dfrac{M}{r^{4}}(r-4M)(r-12M),

when
\ell=4M
. The identity (
61
) follows by putting together the identities (
62
) and (
63
).
∎

We note that the term in the RHS of (
61
) satisfies

\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big|_{r=4M}<0.

This observation shows the local expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow near the unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. By the identity (
61
), the Lyapunov exponent of
(4M,0)
can also be written as

\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(4M)=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}=\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2r^{\frac{3}{2}}_{-}(4M)}\Big(\frac{12M}{r_{-}(4M)}-1\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r_{-}(4M)}\Big).

Remark 5.1.3
.

The zero sets of the functions
\psi_{+}^{4M}
and
\psi_{-}^{4M}
define the unstable and stable manifolds associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(4M)
, respectively. In other words, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(4M)
can be written as

(64)

W^{\pm}(4M)=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=4M,\quad\psi_{\mp}(4M)(x,p)=0\Big\}.

Note the contrast between this property compared to the zero sets
\{\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}=0\}
in Remark
5.1.2
.

Proposition 5.1.9
.

Let
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M})
. There exists
\epsilon>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{|r-4M|\leq\epsilon\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{-}(4M)
, we have

(65)

|r(t(s))-4M|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)t(s))},\qquad|p^{r}(t(s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)t(s))},

for all
s\in[0,a]
. Moreover, for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[-a,0]\to\{|r-4M|\leq\epsilon\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(4M)
, we have

(66)

|r(t(-s))-4M|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)|t(-s)|)},\qquad|p^{r}(t(-s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)|t(-s)|)},

for all
s\in[-a,0]
.

Proof.

We suppose first that
(x,p)\in W^{-}(4M)
. By the invariance of the stable manifold
W^{-}(4M)
, we have that
(x(s),p(s))\in W^{-}(4M)
for all
s\geq 0
. By the characterisation (
64
) of the stable manifolds, we have that
\{\psi_{-}^{\ell}=0\}
, so

p^{r}=\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{4M}{r}\Big).

As a result, the defining function
\psi_{+}^{4M}
satisfies

(67)

\psi_{+}^{4M}(x,p)=2p^{r}=2\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{4M}{r}\Big).

Next, we integrate the derivative (
61
) along the geodesic flow by

\psi_{+}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)=\psi_{+}^{4M}(x,p)\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where here
(x(0)=x,\,p(0)=p)
. Let us set
\epsilon>0
small enough so that

\Big|\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\Big|\leq\delta,

for all
(x,p)\in\{|r-4M|\leq\epsilon\}
. As a result, we have the upper bound

\displaystyle\Big|\psi_{+}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big).

Finally, we use the relations (
67
) to show that

|r(t(s))-4M|+|p^{r}(t(s))|\lesssim\Big|\psi_{+}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big),

where we have used lower bounds for the factors in (
67
). The case of a geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(4M)
follows similarly.
∎

5.1.4.
Expansion and contraction of the radial flow with
\ell\in(4M,\infty)

Let us set the function
H_{\ell}\colon[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

H_{\ell}(E):=\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}-\frac{2M}{E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-1},

with
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
. Note that
H_{\ell}(E)
is conserved along the geodesic flow when the angular momentum is
\ell\geq 0
. By Proposition
5.1.1
, the union
W^{+}(\ell)\,\cup\,W^{-}(\ell)
of the stable manifolds associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, is characterised as
\{H_{\ell}(E)=0\}
.

It will be convenient to parametrise timelike geodesics by the time coordinate
\bar{t}=s(E^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}
in the domain
\{E>1\}
. We define the derivative along the geodesic flow with respect to
\bar{t}
as
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{t}}=(E^{2}-1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
.

Proposition 5.1.10
.

Let us consider the radial flow with
l>4M
. The quantity
H_{\ell}(E)
satisfies

(68)

H_{\ell}(E)=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{E^{2}-1}-\Big(1+\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{2}\Big),

where

a(\ell)=\frac{2M^{2}r_{-}(\ell)^{3}}{\ell^{2}(4M-r_{-}(\ell))(r_{-}(\ell)-3M)}.

Set the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\,\cap\,\{E>1\}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}(x,p):=\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\frac{p^{r}}{(E^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\pm\Big(1+\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big).

Moreover, the derivative of
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
along the geodesic flow is

(69)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{t}}=\pm\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{+}(\ell))}{2(r+a(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}.

Proof.

For
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
, we have
a(\ell)>0
by definition. Let
\bar{r}_{+}(\ell)>\bar{r}_{-}(\ell)>2M
be the roots of
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}
. The functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
are well-defined on the set
\{\bar{r}_{-}<r<\bar{r}_{+}\}
, where the polynomial
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}
is positive. The identities (
68
) and (
69
) follow by the proof of Proposition
5.1.6
.
∎

We note that the term in the RHS of (
69
) satisfies

\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{+}(\ell))}{2(r+a(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\Big|_{r=r_{-}(\ell)}<0.

This shows the local expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow near the unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. By the identity (
69
), the Lyapunov exponent of
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
can also be written as

(70)

\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)=\frac{(E^{2}_{-}(\ell)-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2E_{-}(\ell)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))(r_{-}(\ell)-2M)}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}_{-}(a(\ell)+r_{-}(\ell))^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}_{-}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2})}.

Remark 5.1.4
.

The zero sets of the functions
\varphi_{+}^{\ell}
and
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
define the unstable and stable manifolds associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, respectively. In other words, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
can be written as

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad\varphi_{\mp}^{\ell}(x,p)=0\Big\}.

This characterisation of the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(\ell)
holds similarly as in the case when
\ell=4M
.

5.2.
Degenerate trapping at ISCO

The unique sphere in
\mathcal{P}
containing geodesics with
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
is
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
. This property contrasts with the case of geodesics with
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
, which can be contained in two different spheres. On the other hand, there are no geodesics contained in spheres of fixed radii when
\ell<2\sqrt{3}M
. These properties hold because of a
bifurcation of the radial dynamics at

\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
.

We recall that the fixed point
(6M,0)
of the radial flow with
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
is not hyperbolic, since
\Phi_{2\sqrt{3}M}(6M)=0
. In spite of this, there are still suitable stable and unstable manifolds
W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)
associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
. The submanifolds
W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)
are contained on the energy level
\{E=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}\}
of the radial flow when
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. We parametrise the radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
of future-trapped and past-trapped geodesics with
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
and
E=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
, by

p^{r,-}_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r):=\dfrac{1}{3}\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p^{r,+}_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r):=-\dfrac{1}{3}\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}},

respectively. We summarise this discussion with the following proposition.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

The stable manifolds of the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
are analytic codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
, given by

\displaystyle W^{+}(2\sqrt{3}M)=~

\displaystyle\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=2\sqrt{3}M,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,+}_{2\sqrt{3}M}\Big\},

\displaystyle W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)=~

\displaystyle\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=2\sqrt{3}M,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,-}_{2\sqrt{3}M}\Big\}.

In particular, the intersection
W^{+}(2\sqrt{3}M)\,\cap\,W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
is equal to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
.

By definition, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)
are defined in the region
\{r\leq 6M\}
.

5.2.1.
Expansion and contraction of the radial flow with
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M

Let us set the function
H_{2\sqrt{3}M}\colon[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E):=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-18M.

Note that
H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E)
is conserved along the geodesic flow when
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. The union
W^{+}(2\sqrt{3}M)\,\cup\,W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
of the stable manifolds associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, is characterised as
\{H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E)=0\}
.

Proposition 5.2.2
.

Consider the radial flow with
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. The conserved quantity
H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E)
satisfies

(71)

H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E)=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2}}\Big(\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}-\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{3}\Big).

Set the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\,\cap\,\{E<1\}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}(x,p):=\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\frac{p^{r}}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mp\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big).

Then, the derivative of
\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}
along the geodesic flow is

(72)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{t}}=\pm\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(6M-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})}\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}.

Proof.

We first note that the functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{2\sqrt{3}M}
are well-defined on
\{r\leq 6M\}
since

r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2}=(r-3M)^{2}+3M^{2}\geq 3M^{2}.

The identities (
71
) and (
72
) follow by the proof of Proposition
5.1.6
.
∎

We observe that the term in the RHS of (
72
) satisfies

(73)

\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(6M-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})}>0,

whenever
r<6M
. This shows the local expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow near the unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. However, the expansion and contraction properties
degenerate
, since the function in (
73
) vanishes at
r=6M
.

Remark 5.2.1
.

The zero sets of the functions
\varphi_{+}^{2\sqrt{3}M}
and
\varphi_{-}^{2\sqrt{3}M}
define the unstable and stable manifolds associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, respectively. In other words, the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)
can be written as

(74)

W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=2\sqrt{3}M,\quad\varphi_{\mp}^{2\sqrt{3}M}(x,p)=0\Big\}.

For the fixed point
(r=6M,\,p^{r}=0)
, the rate of contraction and expansion for the geodesic flow on the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(6M)
is of the form
t^{3}
. We proceed to show this rate of contraction and expansion in a neighbourhood of
\{r=6M,\,p^{r}=0\}
.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{5M\leq r\leq 6M\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, we have

(75)

|r(t(s))-6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{3}(s)},\qquad\quad|p^{r}(t(s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{3}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[-a,0]\to\{5M\leq r\leq 6M\}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, we have

(76)

|r(t(-s))-6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|t^{3}(-s)|},\qquad\quad|p^{r}(t(-s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{|t^{3}(-s)|},\qquad\forall s\in[-a,0].

Proof.

We suppose first that
(x,p)\in W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
. By the invariance of the stable manifold
W^{-}(4M)
, we have that
(x(s),p(s))\in W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
for all
s\geq 0
. By the characterisation (
74
) of the stable manifolds, we have that
\{\varphi_{+}^{2\sqrt{3}M}=0\}
, so

(77)

3p^{r}=\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}},

since
E=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}.
As a result, the defining function
\varphi_{-}^{2\sqrt{3}M}
satisfies that

\varphi_{-}^{2\sqrt{3}M}(x,p)=2\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r)\frac{p^{r}}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}=2\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r)\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}},

where
\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r)=r^{\frac{3}{2}}(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is a positive radial weight.

If
(x,p)\in W^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, then
\gamma
is outgoing. So, by integrating the relation (
77
), we obtain

(78)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \dfrac{s}{3}&=\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(6M-r)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{d}r\\
&=-18M\arcsin{\sqrt{\dfrac{r(s)}{6M}}}+\dfrac{\sqrt{r(s)}(18M-r(s))}{\sqrt{6M-r(s)}}+18M\arcsin{\sqrt{\dfrac{r(0)}{6M}}}-\dfrac{\sqrt{r(0)}(18M-r(0))}{\sqrt{6M-r(0)}}\\
&\lesssim 1+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6M-r(s)}},\end{aligned}

Moreover, we have the lower bound

s=\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{p^{t}}=\frac{3}{2\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\gtrsim t(s),

since
E=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
. Therefore, we have

(79)

p^{r}(t(s))=\dfrac{(6M-r(t(s)))^{\frac{3}{2}}}{3r^{\frac{3}{2}}(t(s))}\lesssim t^{-3}(s),

for all
s\geq 0
. A similar argument treats the case of a geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
with
(x,p)\in W^{+}(2\sqrt{3}M)
.
∎

5.3.
Parabolic trapping at infinity

For all
\ell\geq 0
, the radial potential
V_{\ell}
has a local maximum at infinity. The maximum at infinity takes the value one, which corresponds to the rest mass of the particles in the systems we consider. The radial geodesic equation (
24
) shows the existence of
orbits at infinity
contained in the spheres

\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell):=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad r=\infty,\quad p^{r}=0\Big\}.

These orbits have particle energy
E=1
, by the mass-shell relation. We call
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)

spheres of trapped orbits at infinity
, and the orbits they contain
trapped orbits at infinity
.

We will show that the fixed point
(\sqrt{2M}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}=0,\,p^{r}=0)
of the radial flow is
parabolic
. Furthermore, we will identify suitable stable and unstable manifolds
W^{\pm}_{1}(\ell)
associated to the sphere of trapped orbits at infinity
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
. The submanifolds
W^{\pm}_{1}(\ell)
are contained on the energy level
\{E=1\}
. We parametrise the momentum coordinate
p^{r}
of future-trapped and past-trapped orbits at infinity, by

p_{\ell,1}^{r,-}(r):=\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p^{r,+}_{\ell,1}(r):=-\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

respectively.

We summarise this discussion with the following proposition.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
\ell\in[0,4M]
. The stable manifolds of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
at infinity are analytic codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
, given by

\displaystyle W_{1}^{+}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,+}_{\ell,1}\Big\},

\displaystyle W_{1}^{-}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p_{\ell,1}^{r,-}\Big\}.

Moreover, the intersection
W_{1}^{+}(\ell)\,\cap\,W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
is equal to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
when
\ell>4M
, and the intersection
W_{1}^{+}(4M)\,\cap\,W_{1}^{-}(4M)
is equal to
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(4M)\cup\mathcal{S}(4M)
.

If
\ell\in[0,4M)
, the stable manifolds
W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)
are contained in the unbounded region
\{r>2M\}
. By definition of
p^{r,\pm}_{\ell,1}
, we have
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\geq 0
for all
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)
. Furthermore, if
\ell\in[0,4M)
, we have

\forall r>2M,\qquad r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}=\Big(r-\frac{\ell^{2}}{4M}\Big)^{2}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{16M^{2}}(16M^{2}-\ell^{2})\geq 0.

Thus,
W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)
are indeed contained in the unbounded region
\{r>2M\}
. In contrast, if
\ell=4M
, then the stable manifolds
W_{1}^{\pm}(4M)
are only contained in
\{r\geq 4M\}
. This property holds since
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}=(r-4M)^{2}
if
\ell=4M
.

Proposition 5.3.2
.

Let
\ell>4M
. The stable manifolds of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
at infinity, are analytic codimension two submanifolds of
\mathcal{P}
, given by

\displaystyle W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,+}_{\ell,1}\Big\}\cup\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad p^{r}=p^{r,-}_{\ell,1}\Big\},

In particular, the stable manifolds
W_{1}^{+}(\ell)
and
W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
are equal.

If
\ell>4M
, the stable manifolds
W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)
are only contained in
\{r\geq\bar{r}_{+}(\ell)\}
, where
\bar{r}_{+}(\ell)
is the larger root of the polynomial
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}
.

Remark 5.3.1
.

For
\ell>4M
, all the orbits in the stable manifolds are of
homoclinic type
. In other words, these orbits satisfy that
\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}(r(s),p^{r}(s))=(\infty,0)
. Thus, the sets of future-trapped and past-trapped orbits at infinity are both equal, and characterised as
\{E(x,p)=1\}
.

5.3.1.
Parabolic behaviour at infinity

Let us show the parabolic behaviour of the fixed point
(\sqrt{2M}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}=0,\,p^{r}=0)
at infinity. For this purpose, we will consider the
McGehee type coordinates

x=\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r}},\qquad y=p^{r}.

This coordinate system for the radial flow takes infinity to the origin. Furthermore, it allows us to analyse the behaviour of the radial flow in a neigbourhood of the spheres
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
at infinity. Similar coordinates were first introduced by McGehee
[
McG
]
for the study of stable manifolds for parabolic fixed points at infinity with applications to celestial mechanics.

Proposition 5.3.3
.

Consider the radial flow with angular momentum
\ell
. The geodesic flow for the McGehee type coordinates is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}s}=-\frac{x^{3}}{4M}\partial_{y}K_{l}=-\frac{x^{3}y}{4M},\qquad\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}s}=\frac{x^{3}}{4M}\partial_{x}K_{l}=\frac{x^{3}}{4M}\Big(-x+\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M^{2}}x^{3}-\frac{3\ell^{2}}{4M^{2}}x^{5}\Big),

where

K_{l}(x,y):=\frac{y^{2}}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{8M^{2}}(x^{4}-x^{6})=\frac{E^{2}-1}{2}.

Proof.

First, the derivative of
x
along the geodesic flow is

\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}s}=\sqrt{2M}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}})=-\frac{1}{2r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p^{r}=-\frac{x^{3}y}{4M}.

The derivative of
y
along the geodesic flow is exactly the radial geodesic equation, so

\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}s}=-\dfrac{M}{r^{4}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)=-\frac{M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}+3\ell^{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big)=\frac{x^{3}}{4M}\Big(-x+\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M^{2}}x^{3}-\frac{3\ell^{2}}{4M^{2}}x^{5}\Big).

By the mass-shell relation, the conserved quantity
\frac{E^{2}-1}{2}
is equal to

\frac{E^{2}-1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}(p^{r})^{2}-\frac{M}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big)=\frac{y^{2}}{2}-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+\frac{\ell^{2}}{8M^{2}}(x^{4}-x^{6}).

By a direct computation of the partial derivatives of
K_{l}(x,y)
, we obtain the final identities.
∎

5.3.2.
Expansion and contraction of the radial flow with
E=1

Let us consider the conserved quantity along the geodesic flow given by

E^{2}-1.

By Proposition
5.3.1
, the union
W^{+}_{1}(\ell)\,\cup\,W^{-}_{1}(\ell)
of the stable manifolds associated to the sphere at infinity
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
, is characterised as
\{E^{2}-1=0\}
.

Proposition 5.3.4
.

Let us consider the radial flow with
\ell\geq 0
. The quantity
E^{2}-1
satisfies

(80)

E^{2}-1=(p^{r})^{2}-\dfrac{2M}{r^{3}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)

Set the functions
\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}(x,p):=p^{r}\mp\dfrac{(2M)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then, the derivative of
\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}
along the geodesic flow is

(81)

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}s}=\mp\frac{\sqrt{2M}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})}{2r^{\frac{5}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}.

Proof.

The identity (
80
) follows directly by the mass-shell relation. We note that the functions
\psi_{\pm}^{\ell}
are well-defined whenever
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\geq 0
. Furthermore, as long as
r
is sufficiently large, we have
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\geq 0
for all
\ell\geq 0
. We finally show (
81
). We first compute the derivative

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{3}}\Big(\dfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

(82)

\displaystyle=-\frac{p^{r}}{2r^{\frac{5}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big).

We obtain (
81
) by the radial geodesic equation and (
82
).
∎

We observe that the term in the RHS of (
81
) satisfies

(83)

\frac{\sqrt{2M}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2})}{2r^{\frac{5}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}>0,

for
r
sufficiently large in terms of
\ell
. This shows the local expansion and contraction properties of the geodesic flow near the parabolic unstable and stable manifolds, respectively. However, the expansion and contraction properties
degenerate at infinity
, since the function (
83
) vanishes at
r=\infty
. This degeneracy is due to the parabolic behaviour of the fixed point
(\sqrt{2M}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}=0,\,p^{r}=0)
.

Remark 5.3.2
.

The zero sets of the functions
\psi_{+}^{\ell}
and
\psi_{-}^{\ell}
define subsets of the parabolic stable and unstable manifolds associated to the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{\infty}(\ell)
, respectively. For all
\ell\in[0,4M]
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad\psi_{\mp}^{\ell}(x,p)=0\Big\}.

On the other hand, for all
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1,\textrm{in/out}}^{\pm}(\ell)

\displaystyle:=\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}:\ell(x,p)=\ell,\quad\psi_{\mp}^{\ell}(x,p)=0\Big\}\subset W_{1}^{\pm}(\ell).

We note that the geodesics in the subsets
W^{\pm}_{1,\mathrm{in/out}}(\ell)
of the stable manifolds are always either ingoing or outgoing. In particular, the sets
W^{\pm}_{\mathrm{in/out}}(\ell)
only contain the ingoing or the outgoing part of the homoclinic orbits in the energy level
\{E=1\}
when
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
.

For the parabolic fixed point
(\sqrt{2M}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}=0,\,p^{r}=0)
, the rate of contraction and expansion for the geodesic flow on the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}_{1}(\ell)
is given by
t^{\frac{1}{3}}
. We proceed to show this behaviour using the McGehee type coordinates
(\sqrt{2M}r^{-\frac{1}{2}},p^{r})
.

Proposition 5.3.5
.

Let
R>2M
. For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r\geq R\}
with
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
, we have

\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r(t(s))}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\quad|p^{r}(t(s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r\geq R\}
with
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{+}(\ell)
, we have

\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r(t(-s))}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|t^{\frac{1}{3}}(-s)|},\qquad\quad|p^{r}(t(-s))|\lesssim\frac{1}{|t^{\frac{1}{3}}(-s)|},\qquad\forall s\in[-a,0].

Proof.

We suppose first that
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
. By the invariance of the stable manifold
W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
, we have that
(x(s),p(s))\in W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
for all
s\geq 0
. We first consider the case of radial geodesics, i.e. orbits with
\ell=0
. If
\ell=0
and
E=1
, then the mass-shell relation (
23
) is given by
(p^{r})^{2}=\frac{2M}{r}.
We recall that
\gamma
is outgoing if
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{-}(0)
. So, we can integrate the mass-shell relation by

\frac{2}{3}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)-\frac{2}{3}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(0)=\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\sqrt{r}\mathrm{d}r=\sqrt{2M}s.

Furthermore, we have the lower bound

s=\sqrt{2M}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{p^{t}}=\sqrt{2M}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\gtrsim t(s),

since
E=1
. As a result, we have
t(s)\lesssim r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)
, so

(84)

\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r(s)}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)}.

The decay of
p^{r}
follows then, by using the identity
(p^{r})^{2}=\frac{2M}{r}.
The argument works similarly if
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{+}(0)
instead.

Next, we consider the general case of geodesics with
\ell>0.
If
E=1
, then the mass-shell relation (
23
) can be written as
(p^{r})^{2}=\frac{2M}{r^{3}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}).
We recall that
\gamma
is outgoing if
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{-}(\ell)
. So, we can integrate the mass-shell relation by

\frac{2}{3}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)-\frac{2}{3}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(0)\gtrsim\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{d}r=\sqrt{2M}s.

Furthermore, we have the lower bound

s=\sqrt{2M}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{p^{t}}=\sqrt{2M}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\gtrsim t(s),

since
E=1
. As a result, we have
t(s)\lesssim r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)
, so

(85)

\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r(s)}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)}.

The decay of
p^{r}
follows then, by using
(p^{r})^{2}=\frac{2M}{r^{3}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}).
The argument works similarly if
(x,p)\in W_{1}^{+}(\ell)
instead.
∎

6.
Concentration estimates on the stable manifolds

In this section, we prove concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the three forms of trapping on
\mathcal{D}
: unstable trapping, degenerate trapping at ISCO, and parabolic trapping at infinity. These estimates will be obtained by integrating the radial flow in the different regions of the mass-shell, depending on the form of the radial potential
V_{\ell}
. We recall that the mass-shell relation can be written as

(86)

E^{2}=(p^{r})^{2}+V_{\ell}(r),

which can be integrated along the geodesic flow. We decompose the dispersive region
\mathcal{D}
into the three invariant regions

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}:=

\displaystyle~\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}:\ell(x,p)\in[0,2\sqrt{3}M]\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}:=

\displaystyle~\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}:\ell(x,p)\in[2\sqrt{3}M,4M]\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{high}}:=

\displaystyle~\Big\{(x,p)\in\mathcal{D}:\ell(x,p)\in[4M,\infty)\Big\}.

We note that
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}\,\cup\,\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}\,\cup\,\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{high}}
. In the following three subsections, we show concentration estimates on the stable manifolds in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}
, the region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
, and the region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{high}}
, respectively.

6.1.
The region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}

In this region, the radial potential
V_{\ell}
satisfies
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}V_{\ell}\geq 0
for all
r\geq 2M
. Furthermore, the derivative
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}V_{\ell}
only vanishes at
r=6M
when
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. Moreover, the potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has a maximum at infinity where
\lim_{r\to\infty}V_{\ell}(r)=1
. One can easily show that there are only two types of trapping in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}
: parabolic trapping at infinity, and degenerate trapping at ISCO.

6.1.1.
The subregion
E\sim 1

Let us study concentration estimates on the parabolic manifolds at the energy level
\{E=1\}
. Parabolic trapping at infinity holds for all
\ell\in[0,2\sqrt{3}M]
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}
. We will estimate the radial flow in a uniform neighbourhood of the parabolic manifolds, where geodesics spend arbitrarily long periods of time in the far-away region, before crossing
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. We begin considering the case of radial geodesics, in other words, orbits with vanishing angular momentum.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

Let
R>r_{0}>2M
. For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell=0
, particle energy
E\in(\frac{95}{100},1)
, and initial data
(x,p)\in\{r<R\}
, we have

(87)

|1-E^{2}|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{2}{3}}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a],
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{2M}{r(s)}}-p^{r}(s)\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{2M}{r(s)}}+p^{r}(s)\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0.

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

If
\ell=0
, then the mass-shell relation (
86
) can be written as

(88)

1-E^{2}=\frac{2M}{r}-(p^{r})^{2}.

So, for radial geodesics with
E<1
, the radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
vanishes at
r=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}
