. We note that the turning point at
r=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}
moves to infinity as
E\to 1
.

Let us suppose first that
\gamma
is an outgoing geodesic. Integrating the mass-shell relation (
88
), we have

(89)

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=\int_{r(0)}^{\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{r}-(1-E^{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\frac{1}{r}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By using the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}_{0}(r):=r(1-E^{2})
, we have

(90)

\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\frac{1}{r}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\dfrac{1}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{r(0)(1-E^{2})}^{2M}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}_{0}}{(\frac{2M}{\tilde{r}_{0}}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\dfrac{1}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}},

where the estimate above follows by an explicit computation of the integral term. Moreover, the coordinate
p^{v}
satisfies
\Omega^{2}p^{v}\leq E<1
, so

(91)

s=\int_{v_{0}}^{v(s)}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{p^{v}}\geq\int_{v_{0}}^{v(s)}\Omega^{2}(r)\mathrm{d}v\gtrsim v(s),

since
\gamma(s)\in\{r>r_{0}>2M\}
for all
s\in[0,a]
. As a result, we obtain the second estimate in (
87
) by

\Big|\dfrac{2M}{r(s)}-p^{r}(s)^{2}\Big|=|1-E^{2}|\lesssim v^{-\frac{2}{3}}(s),

by putting together (
90
) and (
91
). Finally, we obtain the first estimate in (
87
) by

\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{2M}{r(s)}}-p^{r}(s)\Big|\leq|1-E^{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim v^{-\frac{1}{3}}(s),

where we used the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

Analogous estimates hold when
\gamma
is ingoing instead. For geodesics with a turning point at
r=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}
, we put together the estimates in the case where
\gamma
is outgoing and ingoing. The decay estimates in (
87
) also hold with respect to
u
. For this, we use that
s\gtrsim u(s)
since
\gamma(s)\in\{r>r_{0}>2M\}
for all
s\in[0,a]
.
∎

We extend next Proposition
6.1.1
to the case of geodesics with
\ell\leq 2\sqrt{3}M
. In this regime, we will consider geodesics
\gamma_{x,p}
with particle energy
E\in(\frac{95}{100},1)
, so that we localise the behaviour of the geodesic flow near the parabolic stable manifolds
W^{\pm}_{1}(\ell)
.

Proposition 6.1.2
.

Let
R>r_{0}>12M
. For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell\leq 2\sqrt{3}M
, particle energy
E\in(\frac{95}{100},1)
, and initial data
(x,p)\in\{r<R\}
, we have

(92)

|1-E^{2}|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{2}{3}}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a],
we have

(93)

\displaystyle\Big|\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}-p^{r}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0,

(94)

\displaystyle\Big|\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+p^{r}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0.

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

We first write the mass-shell relation (
86
) as

(95)

1-E^{2}=\dfrac{2M}{r^{3}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)-(p^{r})^{2}.

Let
E<1
. By the form of the radial potential
V_{\ell}
, we define the unique root of
E^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)
as
r_{E}
. The radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
vanishes at
r=r_{E}
. A direct computation shows that

E^{2}-V_{\ell}\Big(\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\Big)<0<E^{2}-V_{\ell}\Big(\frac{M}{1-E^{2}}\Big),

so
r_{E}\in(\frac{M}{1-E^{2}},\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}})
. As a result, the turning point at
r=r_{E}
moves to infinity as
E\to 1
.

We suppose first that
\gamma
is an outgoing geodesic. Integrating the mass-shell relation (
95
), we have

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=\int_{r(0)}^{r_{E}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{r^{3}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})-(1-E^{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{r_{E}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Let us set the function
\tilde{r}_{\ell}\colon[2M,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r):=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}(1-E^{2}).

The function
\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r)
is well-defined on
[2M,\infty)
, since
r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}
is positive when
\ell\leq 2\sqrt{3}M
. In the rest of the proof, we denote
\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r)
simply as
\tilde{r}(r)
. We now compute the derivative

\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{\mathrm{d}r}=r^{2}\frac{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}}{(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{2}}(1-E^{2}).

We note that
(1-E^{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{\mathrm{d}r}(r)>0
for
r>r_{0}>12M
. By using the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}(r)
, we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{r_{E}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\dfrac{1}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{\tilde{r}(0)}^{\tilde{r}(r_{E})}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{(\frac{2M}{\tilde{r}}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\dfrac{1}{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}},

where the estimate above follows by an explicit computation of the integral term. As a result, we obtain the second estimate in (
92
) by

\Big|\dfrac{2M}{r(s)}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}(s)}+\dfrac{2M\ell^{2}}{r^{3}(s)}-p^{r}(s)^{2}\Big|=|1-E^{2}|\lesssim v^{-\frac{2}{3}}(s),

where we have used that
s\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{r>r_{0}\}
. See the estimate (
91
). Finally, the we obtain the estimate (
93
) by

\Big|\sqrt{\dfrac{2M}{r^{3}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)}-p^{r}\Big|\leq|1-E^{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim v^{-\frac{1}{3}}(s),

where we used the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

Analogous estimates hold when
\gamma
is ingoing instead. For geodesics with a turning point at
\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r_{E})
, we put together the estimates in the case where
\gamma
is outgoing and ingoing. The decay estimates in (
92
) also hold with respect to
u
. For this, we use that
s\gtrsim u(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\in\{r>r_{0}>12M\}
.
∎

Remark 6.1.1
.

The decay rate
t^{-{\frac{2}{3}}}
in Proposition
6.1.2
holds because the radial potential
V_{\ell}(r)
satisfies
|V_{\ell}(r)\,-\,1|\sim 2Mr^{-1}
when
r
goes to infinity. More generally, for a radial potential
\Phi(r)
satisfying that
|\Phi(r)\,-\,1|\sim r^{-n}
when
r
goes to infinity, one could show concentration estimates with the decay rate
t^{-\frac{2n}{n+2}}
. This observation is relevant when considering decay estimates for massive Vlasov fields on higher dimensional Schwarzschild black holes.

6.1.2.
The subregion
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M
and
E\sim\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}

Let us study concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the degenerate trapping at ISCO in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}
. We recall that this form of trapping holds for
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
and
E=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
. We will control the radial flow in a uniform neighbourhood of the sphere
\mathcal{S}_{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
. We will first consider the case of geodesics contained in
\{\ell=2\sqrt{3}M\}
. Note that the set
\{\ell=2\sqrt{3}M\}
is located at the boundary of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{low}}
.

Proposition 6.1.3
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{5M<r<7M\}
with angular momentum
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-18M\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{6}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a]
, we have

(96)

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\geq 6M,

(97)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\leq 6M.

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.2.2
, the mass-shell relation (
86
) can be written as

(98)

H_{2\sqrt{3}M}(E):=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-18M=\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}\Big(\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}-\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{3}\Big),

where
\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r):=r^{3}(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{-1}
. For simplicity, we write below
H
and
\omega
, for
H_{2\sqrt{3}M}
and
\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}
, respectively. By the form of the radial potential
V_{\ell}
, we define
r_{H}
as the unique real value such that

H=\frac{(r_{H}-6M)^{3}}{r_{H}^{2}-6Mr_{H}+12M^{2}}.

The radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
vanishes at
r=r_{H}
by (
98
). Moreover, one can show that
r_{H}\in\{5M<r<7M\}
.

We suppose first that
\gamma
is an outgoing geodesic. We will consider three different cases: geodesics in the region
\{r\geq 6M\}
, in the region
\{r\leq 6M,\,H>0\}
, or in the region
\{r\leq 6M,\,H<0\}
.

The region
\{r\geq 6M\}
.
We first note that orbits in
\{r\geq 6M\}
satisfy
H>0
by definition of
H
. Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(0)=6M
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
98
), we have

(99)

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{6M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-(1-\frac{6M}{r})^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{6M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r}{(1-(1-\frac{6M}{r})^{3}\frac{\omega}{H})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

since the geodesic
\gamma
is outgoing.
Let us set the function
\tilde{r}_{2\sqrt{3}M}\colon[2M,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

(100)

\tilde{r}_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r)=6M+\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{r-6M}{(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

The function
\tilde{r}_{2\sqrt{3}M}(r)
is well-defined on
[2M,\infty)
since
r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2}\geq 3M^{2}.
In the rest of the proof, we denote
\tilde{r}_{2\sqrt{3}M}
simply as
\tilde{r}
. We compute the radial derivative

\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{\mathrm{d}r}=\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{3}}}\Big(\frac{1}{(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{\frac{1}{3}}}-\frac{2(r-3M)(r-6M)}{3(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big).

We note that

H^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{\mathrm{d}r}\Big|_{r=6M}=\frac{1}{\sqrt[3]{12M^{2}}}>0.

Furthermore, the derivative
\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{\mathrm{d}r}
is positive in a neighbourhood of
r=6M
.

By using the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}(r)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{6M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r}{(1-(1-\frac{6M}{r})^{3}\frac{\omega}{H})^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{6}}}\int_{6M}^{\tilde{r}(r_{H})}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(1-(\tilde{r}-6M)^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

(101)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{6}}}\int_{6M}^{\tilde{r}(r_{H})}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}{(1-(\tilde{r}-6M)^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{6}}},

where the last estimate follows by an explicit computation of the integral term. As a result, we obtain
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s)
, where we have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

by the positivity of the second term in the definition of
H
in (
98
).

The region
\{r\leq 6M,\,H>0\}
.
Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(a)=6M
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
98
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{r(0)}^{6M}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{((\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}+\frac{H}{\omega})^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{6M}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r}{((\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}\frac{\omega}{H}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By using the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}(r)
, we have

\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{r(0)}^{6M}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r}{((\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}\frac{\omega}{H}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{H^{\frac{1}{6}}}\int_{\tilde{r}(0)}^{\tilde{r}(6M)}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}{((6M-\tilde{r})^{3}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\lesssim H^{-\frac{1}{6}},

where the estimate follows in the same way as in (
101
). As a result, we obtain
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s)
, where we have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

The region
\{r\leq 6M,\,H<0\}
.
Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(0)=5M
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
98
), we have

\bar{t}=\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{((\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}-\frac{(-H)}{\omega})^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{(-H)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{w^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{\omega}{-H}(\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

for all
\bar{t}
. By using the change variables

r\mapsto\tilde{r}(r)=6M+\frac{1}{(-H)^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{r-6M}{(r^{2}-6Mr+12M^{2})^{\frac{1}{3}}},

we have

\frac{1}{(-H)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{\omega}{-H}(\frac{6M}{r}-1)^{3}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{1}{(-H)^{\frac{1}{6}}}\int_{\tilde{r}(5M)}^{\tilde{r}(r_{H})}\frac{\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}{((6M-\tilde{r})^{3}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\lesssim\frac{1}{(-H)^{\frac{1}{6}}}.

As a result, we obtain
\sqrt{-H}\lesssim v^{-3}
, where we have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\Big(\frac{6M}{r}-1\Big)^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big|\lesssim(-H)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega_{2\sqrt{3}M}
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

Analogous estimates hold in the regime where
\gamma
is ingoing. For geodesics with a turning point at
r_{H}
, we put together the estimates when
\gamma
is outgoing and ingoing in the regions considered above. The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
. For this, we use that
s\gtrsim u(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{r>5M\}
.
∎

Next, we study concentration estimates on the stable manifolds for geodesics with angular momentum
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M
, when
\ell
is strictly less than
2\sqrt{3}M
. Let us establish uniform estimates for the geodesic flow as
\ell\to 2\sqrt{3}M
. For this purpose, we will perform estimates for all
\ell<2\sqrt{3}M
near a suitably chosen energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
.

Given
\ell\in[0,2\sqrt{3}M]
, we denote by
r_{E}
the radial value where orbits with angular momentum
\ell
and particle energy
E\in(0,1)
turn, i.e.
p^{r}=0
. In other words, the real value
r_{E}\in(2M,\infty)
satisfies that
E^{2}=V_{\ell}(r_{E})
for every
E\in(0,1)
. The map
E\mapsto r_{E}
is well-defined since
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}V_{\ell}\geq 0
when
\ell\in[0,2\sqrt{3}M]
.

Let us define the function
\Psi_{E}\colon[2M,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\Psi_{E}(r):=r^{3}\Big(V_{\ell}(r_{E})-V_{\ell}(r)\Big).

By definition, we have
\Psi_{E}(r_{E})=0
for every
E\in(0,1)
. Furthermore, we have that
\Psi_{E}(r)=r^{3}(p^{r})^{2}
by the mass-shell relation.

Proposition 6.1.4
.

Let
\ell\in(4\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M]
. The unique continuous function
E_{-}\colon(4\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M]\to[0,\infty)
such that
r_{E_{-}(\ell)}
is a double root of
\Psi_{E_{-}(\ell)}(r)
and
E_{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
, is given by

E_{-}(\ell)=\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}\Big(r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad r_{-}(\ell):=\frac{3\ell^{2}}{8M}\Big(1+\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}\Big).

Moreover, the conserved quantity
H_{\ell}(E):=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}
satisfies

(102)

H_{\ell}(E)=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\Big[\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}+\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)\Big].

Proof.

By the mass-shell relation, we have

\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{E^{2}-1}-1=\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{r-2M}{r^{3}}=\frac{2M}{E^{2}-1}\cdot\frac{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}.

So, the radial value
r_{E}
, where orbits with particle energy
E\in(0,1)
turn, satisfies

(103)

E^{2}=1-\frac{2M}{r_{E}^{3}}\Big(r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}\Big).

Let us consider the cubic polynomial
h_{E}(r)
given by

h_{E}(r):=-\frac{1}{2M}\frac{r^{3}_{E}}{r^{2}_{E}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}}\Psi_{E}(r)=r^{3}-\frac{r^{3}_{E}}{r^{2}_{E}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big).

We write
h_{E}(r)
as a polynomial in
r-r_{E}
by

\displaystyle h_{E}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\Big(r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}\Big)r^{3}-r_{E}^{3}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\Big(r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}\Big)(r^{3}-r_{E}^{3})+r_{E}^{3}(r_{E}-r)\Big(r-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}+r_{E}\Big)\Big)

(104)

\displaystyle=(r-r_{E})\Big((r-r_{E})^{2}+3rr_{E}-\frac{r_{E}^{3}}{r_{E}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{E}+\ell^{2}}\Big(r-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}+r_{E}\Big)\Big).

The condition
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}r^{2}}\Psi_{E_{-}(\ell)}(r_{E_{-}(\ell)})=0
holds if and only if
h^{\prime\prime}_{E_{-}(\ell)}(r_{E_{-}}(\ell))=0
. The latter property is satisfied when the linear term in the second factor of (
104
) vanishes. In other words, we require that

(105)

r_{E_{-}(\ell)}^{2}-\frac{3\ell^{2}}{4M}r_{E_{-}(\ell)}+\frac{3\ell^{2}}{2}=0.

for every
\ell\in(4\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M]
. By the condition
E_{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
, we note that
r_{E_{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)}=6M
. Thus, we choose the regular solution of (
105
) given by

r_{-}(\ell):=\frac{3\ell^{2}}{8M}\Big(1+\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}\Big).

The unique continuous function
E_{-}(\ell)
satisfying the conditions in the statement is obtained by plugging
r_{-}(\ell)
in (
103
). Finally, the mass-shell relation (
86
) can be written in terms of
H_{\ell}(E)
as

H_{\ell}(E)=\frac{r^{3}}{r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}}\Big[\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}+\frac{r-r_{-}(\ell)}{r^{3}}\Big((r-r_{-}(\ell))^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}(\ell)\Big)\Big)\Big].

∎

For our purposes, we will show that the coefficient
b(\ell)\in{\mathbb{R}}
in the last parenthesis of (
102
), given by

b(\ell):=\frac{r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}(\ell)\Big),

is positive for every
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M
with
\ell\neq 2\sqrt{3}M
. We begin proving an elementary lemma about the derivatives of the function
r_{-}(\ell)
.

Lemma 6.1.5
.

The first and second order derivatives of
r_{-}:(4\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M]\to(2M,\infty)
are given by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{4M}{3\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell),

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4M}{\ell^{4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)-\frac{128M^{3}}{9\ell^{6}}\frac{1}{(1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)+\frac{32M^{2}}{9\ell^{6}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}r^{3}_{-}(\ell).

Proof.

We first compute the derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\ell}{4M}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}\Big(\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}+\Big(1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}\Big)+\frac{16M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{3\ell}{8M}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}\Big(1+\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}\Big)^{2}=\frac{4M}{3\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell),

where we used in the last equality the definition of
r_{-}(\ell)
. Next, we compute the second order derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4M}{\ell^{4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)-\frac{128M^{3}}{9\ell^{6}}\frac{1}{(1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)+\frac{4M}{3\ell^{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}2r_{-}(\ell)\frac{\mathrm{d}r_{-}}{\mathrm{d}\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4M}{\ell^{4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)-\frac{128M^{3}}{9\ell^{6}}\frac{1}{(1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}r^{2}_{-}(\ell)+\frac{32M^{2}}{9\ell^{6}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{32M^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}}r^{3}_{-}(\ell).

∎

Let us set the function
c\colon(2\sqrt{2}M,2\sqrt{3}M]\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

c(\ell):=\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}(\ell).

By definition of
b(\ell)
, we have that

b(\ell)=\frac{c(\ell)r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}}.

As a result, the coefficient
b(\ell)
is positive for
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M
with
\ell\neq 2\sqrt{3}M
, if and only if
c(\ell)
is positive for
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M
with
\ell\neq 2\sqrt{3}M
. We observe that
c(2\sqrt{3}M)=0
.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

The function
c(\ell)
has a local minimum at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. In particular, the functions
c(\ell)
and
b(\ell)
are non-negative in an open interval whose right end point is open at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
.

Proof.

By definition of
r_{-}(\ell)
, we have
c(2\sqrt{3}M)=0
. By computing the first and second order derivatives of
c(\ell)
, we have
\frac{\mathrm{d}c}{\mathrm{d}\ell}(2\sqrt{3}M)=0
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}c}{\mathrm{d}\ell^{2}}(2\sqrt{3}M)=\frac{8}{M}>0
. Thus, the function
c(\ell)
has a local minimum at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. In particular, we have
c(\ell)\geq 0
in an open interval whose right end point is open at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. Furthermore, we note that

\frac{r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}}\Big|_{\ell=2\sqrt{3}M}=18M>0.

As a result, the function
b(\ell)
is non-negative in an open interval whose right end point is open at
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
, since

b(\ell)=\frac{r_{-}^{3}(\ell)}{r_{-}^{2}(\ell)-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}(\ell)+\ell^{2}}c(\ell)>0.

∎

We can finally show uniform concentration estimates as
\ell\to 2\sqrt{3}M
.

Proposition 6.1.7
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{5M<r<7M\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{2}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{6}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a]
, we have

(106)

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\quad\text{if}\quad r(s)\geq r_{-}(\ell),

(107)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\frac{(r_{-}-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\quad\text{if}\quad r(s)\leq r_{-}(\ell).

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.1.4
, the mass-shell relation (
86
) can be written as

(108)

H_{\ell}=\omega_{\ell}(r)\Big[\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}+\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)\Big],

where
\omega_{\ell}(r)=r^{3}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{-1}
. For simplicity, we write below
H
and
\omega
, for
H_{\ell}
and
\omega_{\ell}
, respectively. By the form of the radial potential
V_{\ell}
, we define
r_{H}
as the unique real value such that

H=\frac{r_{H}-r_{-}}{r^{2}_{H}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{H}+\ell^{2}}\Big((r_{H}-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big).

The radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
vanishes at
r=r_{H}
by (
108
). Moreover, one can show that
r_{H}\in\{5M<r<7M\}
.

We suppose first that
\gamma
is an outgoing geodesic. We will consider three different cases: geodesics in the region
\{r\geq r_{-}\}
, in the region
\{r\leq r_{-},\,H>0\}
, or in the region
\{r\leq r_{-},\,H<0\}
.

The region
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
.
We first note that orbits in
\{r\geq r_{-}\}
satisfy
H>0
by definition of
H
. Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(0)=r_{-}(\ell)
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
108
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-})^{3}-\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-})\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

since the geodesic
\gamma
is outgoing. Let us set the function
\tilde{r}_{\ell}\colon[2M,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

(109)

\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r):=r_{-}(\ell)+\dfrac{1}{b^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)}(r-r_{-}(\ell)),\quad b(\ell)=\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}.

The function
\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r)
is well-defined on
[2M,\infty)
since
\ell\in(2\sqrt{2}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
. In the rest of the proof, we denote
\tilde{r}_{\ell}
simply as
\tilde{r}
. We note that
\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}_{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}r}=b^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)>0
. By using the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r)
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}((r-r_{-})^{2}+b(\ell)))^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=b^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{\tilde{r}(r_{H})}\Big(\frac{H}{\omega(\tilde{r})}-\frac{b^{\frac{3}{2}}(\ell)(\tilde{r}-r_{-})}{(r_{-}+b^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)(\tilde{r}-r_{-}))^{3}}\Big((\tilde{r}-r_{-})^{2}+1\Big)\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

\displaystyle=b^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\ell)\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{\tilde{r}(r_{H})}\Big(\frac{H}{b^{\frac{3}{2}}(\ell)}-\frac{(\tilde{r}-r_{-})}{(r_{-}+b^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)(\tilde{r}-r_{-}))^{3}}((\tilde{r}-r_{-})^{2}+1)\omega\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}

(110)

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\ell),

where the last inequality holds for
|H|\lesssim b^{\frac{3}{2}}(\ell)
. Thus, we have
\bar{t}\lesssim b^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\ell)\lesssim H^{-\frac{1}{6}}
, so we obtain
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}
. We have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case by

|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

by the positivity of the second term in the definition of
H
in (
108
).

The region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H>0\}
.
Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(0)=5M
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
108
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-})^{3}-\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-})\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Furthermore, we have

\bar{t}\leq\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r_{-}-r)^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

since
r_{-}(\ell)\geq r
. By the estimate (
101
) in Proposition
6.1.3
, we obtain
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}
, where we have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{r_{-}-r}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega_{\ell}
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

The region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H<0\}
.
Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(0)=5M
. Integrating the geodesic equation, we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(-\frac{(-H)}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-})^{3}-\frac{r-r_{-}}{r^{3}}(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-})\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Furthermore, we have

\bar{t}\leq\int_{5M}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(-\frac{(-H)}{\omega}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r_{-}-r)^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

since
r_{-}(\ell)\geq r
. By the estimate (
101
) in Proposition
6.1.3
, we obtain
\sqrt{-H}\lesssim v^{-3}
, where we have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{r_{-}-r}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{-H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

Analogous estimates hold in the regime where
\gamma
is ingoing. For geodesics with a turning point at
r_{H}
, we put together the estimates when
\gamma
is outgoing and ingoing in the regions considered above. The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
. For this, we use that
s\gtrsim u(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{r>5M\}
.
∎

Remark 6.1.2
.

The decay rate
t^{-3}
in Proposition
6.2.2
holds, because of the cubic potential
(1-\frac{6M}{r})^{3}
in the geodesic equation (
71
). More generally, for a radial potential of the form
(1-\frac{r_{c}}{r})^{n}
with
r_{c}>0
, one can show concentration estimates with the decay rate
t^{-\frac{n}{n-2}}
.

6.2.
The region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}

In this region, the radial potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has critical points at
r=r_{\pm}(\ell)
, where
V_{\ell}(r)
has a local maximum at
r_{-}(\ell)
, and a local minimum at
r_{+}(\ell)
. Moreover, the potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has a maximum at infinity where
\lim_{r\to\infty}V_{\ell}(r)=1
. We remark that
V_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell))<1
, so the fixed point
(r=r_{-}(\ell),\,p^{r}=0)
of the radial flow is a homoclinic point. One can easily show that there are three types of trapping in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
: parabolic trapping at infinity, degenerate trapping at ISCO, and unstable trapping.

6.2.1.
The subregion
E\sim 1

Let us study concentration estimates on the parabolic manifolds at the energy level
\{E=1\}
. Parabolic trapping at infinity holds for all
\ell\in[2\sqrt{3}M,4M]
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
. We will estimate the radial flow in a uniform neighbourhood of the parabolic manifolds.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
R>r_{0}>16M
. For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>r_{0}\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
, particle energy
E\in[E_{-}(\ell),1)
, and initial data
(x,p)\in\{16M<r<R\}
, we have

|1-E^{2}|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{2}{3}}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a],
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}-p^{r}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\Big|\dfrac{\sqrt{2M}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(r^{2}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+p^{r}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0.

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is similar to the proof of Proposition
6.1.2
. Here, we consider
R>r_{0}>16M
instead, so that we obtain uniform bounds for
\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}_{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}r}
after performing the change of variables
r\mapsto\tilde{r}_{\ell}(r)
. Moreover, we consider geodesics with initial data
(x,p)\in\{16M<r<R\}
to avoid making any reference to the flow near
\{r=r_{-}(\ell)\}
for
\ell\sim 4M
. The rest of the proof follows without further changes.
∎

6.2.2.
The subregion
E\sim\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}
with
\ell\sim 2\sqrt{3}M

Let us study concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the degenerate trapping at ISCO in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
. We will estimate the radial flow in a uniform neighbourhood of the sphere
\mathcal{S}_{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
. The set
\{\ell=2\sqrt{3}M\}
is located at the boundary of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
.

Proposition 6.2.2
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{5M<r<7M\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{6}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a]
, we have

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad r\in[-a(\ell),\infty),

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0\quad\text{ and }\quad r\in[r_{-}(\ell),-a(\ell)],

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}+\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0\quad\text{ and }\quad r\in(5M,-a(\ell)].

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.1.6
, the mass-shell relation (
86
) can be written as

(111)

H_{\ell}(E):=\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}-\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}=\omega_{\ell}(r)\Big(\frac{(p^{r})^{2}}{1-E^{2}}+\Big(1+\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{2}\Big),

where
\omega_{\ell}(r):=r^{3}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})^{-1}
. For simplicity, we write below
H
and
\omega
, for
H_{\ell}
and
\omega_{\ell}
, respectively. By the form of the radial potential
V_{\ell}
, we define
r_{H}
as the unique real value such that

H=\frac{(r_{H}+a(\ell))(r_{H}-r_{-}(\ell))}{r^{2}_{H}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{H}+\ell^{2}}.

The radial momentum coordinate
p^{r}
vanishes at
r=r_{H}
by (
111
). Moreover, one can show that
r_{H}\in\{5M<r<7M\}
.

We suppose first that
\gamma
is an outgoing geodesic. We will consider three different cases: geodesics in the region
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
, in the region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H>0\}
, or in the region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H<0\}
.

The region
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
.
We first note that orbits in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bd}}
satisfy
H\geq 0
. In general, orbits in
\{r\geq r_{-}(\ell)\}
can also have
H<0
, however, this only occurs in the region
{\mathcal{P}}\setminus\mathcal{D}
of the mass-shell. Without loss of generality, we consider the case where
r(0)=r_{-}(\ell)
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
111
), we have

(112)

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-})^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

since the geodesic
\gamma
is outgoing. We note that

(113)

(r+a(\ell))(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{2}=(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{3}-(-a(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{2},

where
-a(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell)>0
for
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,2\sqrt{3}M+\frac{1}{2}M]
. Thus, we can bound

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{3}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(-a(\ell)-r_{-}(\ell))(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r_{-}(\ell)}^{r_{H}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By using the estimate (
101
) performed in Proposition
6.1.3
, we obtain that
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}
. We have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
.
Thus, for every
r\geq-a(\ell)
, we have

|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

by the positivity of the second term in the definition of
H
in (
111
). Otherwise, if
r\leq-a(\ell)
, then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}-\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}>0,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}+\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}<0,

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

The region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H>0\}
.

Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(a)=r_{-}(\ell)
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
111
) and using (
113
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{5M}^{r_{-}(\ell)}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(\frac{H}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By using the estimate (
101
) performed in Proposition
6.1.3
, we obtain that
\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}
. We have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}+\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega_{\ell}
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

The region
\{r\leq r_{-}(\ell),\,H<0\}
.

Without loss of generality, we consider the case when
r(a)=r_{-}(\ell)
. Integrating the geodesic equation (
111
) and using (
113
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{5M}^{r_{-}(\ell)}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(-\frac{(-H)}{\omega}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}(r-r_{-}(\ell))^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By using the estimate (
101
) performed in Proposition
6.1.3
, we obtain that
\sqrt{-H}\lesssim v^{-3}
. We have used that
\bar{t}\gtrsim v(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{5M<r<7M\}
. We conclude this case since

\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}+\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{-H}\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used a lower bound for
\omega
, and the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder continuity of the square root.

Analogous estimates hold in the regime where
\gamma
is ingoing. For geodesics with a turning point at
r=r_{H}
, we put together the estimates when
\gamma
is outgoing and ingoing. The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
. For this, we use that
s\gtrsim u(s)
since
\gamma([0,a])\subset\{r>5M\}
.
∎

6.2.3.
The subregion
E\sim E_{-}(\ell)

Let us study concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the unstable trapping at the energy level
\{E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell)\}
for
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
. We will estimate the radial flow in a neighbourhood of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
.

Proposition 6.2.3
.

Let
\delta>0
. There exists
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2}M)
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M+\epsilon,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(E_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon,E_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon)
, we have

(114)

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s))},\qquad\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp((\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta)t(s))}

for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

We first note that the Lyapunov exponents of
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
satisfy

\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)\geq\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(2\sqrt{3}M+\epsilon)>0,

by the monotonicity property of
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
in Proposition
5.1.5
. We set
\epsilon>0
small enough so that

\Big|\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\frac{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\Big|\leq\delta,

for all
(x,p)\in\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}\cap\{|E-E_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}
. Next, we integrate the derivative along the geodesic flow of
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
by

(115)

\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)=\varphi_{-}^{\ell}(x,p)\exp\Big(-\frac{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where
(x(0)=x,p(0)=p)
. As a result, we have the bound

|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\mathrm{d}t\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big),

so the momentum coordinate
p^{r}
satisfies

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big).

Finally, we note that
\varphi_{+}^{\ell}
is bounded in the region
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}\cap\{|E-E_{-}(\ell)|<{\epsilon}\}
. Thus,

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|=\Big|\varphi_{+}^{\ell}\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big).

∎

Similarly, we show concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the unstable trapping at the energy level
\{E=1\}
for
\ell=4M
. In this case, we consider the defining functions
\psi_{\pm}^{4M}
for the stable manifolds. We treat this case apart because the defining functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{\ell}
degenerate at
E=1
.

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Let
\delta>0
. There exists
\epsilon>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{|r-4M|<\epsilon\}
with angular momentum
\ell=4M
and particle energy
E\in(1-\epsilon,1+\epsilon)
, we have

(116)

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)t(s))},\qquad|E^{2}\,-\,1|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp((\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta)t(s))}

for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

We set
\epsilon>0
small enough so that

\Big|\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2E}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\Big|\leq\delta,

for all
(x,p)\in\{|r-4M|\leq\epsilon\}\cap\{|E-E_{-}(\ell)|<{\epsilon}\}.
We integrate the derivative along the geodesic flow of
\psi_{+}^{4M}
by

\psi_{+}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)=\psi_{+}^{4M}(x,p)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{E}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where
(x(0)=x,p(0)=p)
. As a result, we have

|\psi_{+}^{4M}|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{E}\int_{0}^{t(s)}\frac{\sqrt{2M}}{2}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(1-\frac{12M}{r}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{2M}{r}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big),

so the momentum coordinate
p^{r}
satisfies

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\Big|\psi_{+}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big).

Finally, we note that
\psi_{-}^{4M}
is bounded in the region
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}\cap\{|E-E_{-}(\ell)|<{\epsilon}\}
. Thus,

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|=\Big|\psi_{+}^{4M}\psi_{-}^{4M}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}-\delta\Big)t(s)\Big).

∎

Remark 6.2.1
.

We observe that Propositions
6.2.3
and
6.2.4
, could have been obtained by using the quantitative estimates for the geodesic flow that follow from the proof of the stable manifold theorem. See
[
KH95
, Theorem 17.4.3]
for more details about the standard proof of the stable manifold theorem, based on the
graph transform
over the stable and unstable subspaces defined by the linearised flow. Instead, we have performed a proof that makes use of the explicit defining functions of the stable manifolds associated to the trapped set
\Gamma
of Schwarzschild. This strategy gives more information about the concentration of the geodesic flow on the unstable manifold in the specific setup of Schwarzschild spacetime.

For our purposes, we will require concentration estimates near the whole homoclinic orbits at the energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
. The following estimates do not only consider geodesics in a neighbourhood of the sphere
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
as in Propositions
6.2.3
and
6.2.4
.

Proposition 6.2.5
.

Let
R>2M
. For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{5M<r<R\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{6}(s)},\qquad\forall s\in[0,a].

Moreover, for all
s\in[0,a]
, we have

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad r\in[-a(\ell),\infty),

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\,-\,\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0\quad\text{ and }\quad r\in[r_{-}(\ell),-a(\ell)],

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\,+\,\Big(-1-\frac{a(\ell)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r_{-}(\ell)}{r}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0\quad\text{ and }\quad r\in(5M,-a(\ell)].

The same decay estimates hold with respect to the time coordinate
u
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is similar to the proof of Proposition
6.2.2
. We consider here geodesics with a larger range of possible angular momentum values. By the assumption that the particle energy
E
is in
(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we only consider unstable trapping in a range
(2\sqrt{3}M,L_{1})
with
L_{1}<4M
. Because of this property, the function
-a(\ell)\geq 2M
satisfies the uniform bound
-a(\ell)\leq A
for a constant
A>2M
. As a result, the corresponding homoclinic orbits are contained in the bounded region
\{-a(\ell)\leq A\}
. By these considerations, the proof can be adapted from the arguments in Proposition
6.2.2
.
∎

Remark 6.2.2
.

We remark that Proposition
6.2.5
also applies to geodesics with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30},1)
in the bounded region. We also note that this result does not apply in the far-away region, because the homoclinic orbits become larger and larger as
\ell\to 4M
, so the constant in the RHS of the estimates degenerates.

6.3.
The region
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{high}}

In this region, the radial potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has critical points at
r=r_{\pm}(\ell)
, where
V_{\ell}(r)
has a local maximum at
r_{-}(\ell)
, and a local minimum at
r_{+}(\ell)
. Moreover, the potential
V_{\ell}(r)
has a maximum at infinity where
\lim_{r\to\infty}V_{\ell}(r)=1
. We remark that
V_{\ell}(r_{-}(\ell))>1
, so the fixed point
(r=r_{-}(\ell),\,p^{r}=0)
of the radial flow is
not a homoclinic point
. One can easily show that there is only one type of trapping in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{high}}
: unstable trapping.

6.3.1.
The subregion
E\sim E_{-}(\ell)

Let us study concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the unstable trapping effect at the energy level
\{E(x,p)=E_{-}(\ell)\}
for
\ell\in(4M,\infty)
. We will estimate the radial flow in a neighbourhood of the spheres
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
for all
\ell\geq\ell_{0}>4M
for a fixed
\ell_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Let
\ell_{0}>4M
. There exists
{\epsilon}>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}
with angular momentum
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
and particle energy
E\in(E_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon,E_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon)
, we have

(117)

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}t(s))},\qquad\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{\exp(\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}t(s))},

for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

We first note that the Lyapunov exponents of the fixed points
(r_{-}(\ell),0)
satisfy

(118)

\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)\geq\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell_{0})\geq\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(4M)=\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M},

by the monotonicity property of
\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)
in Proposition
5.1.5
. We set
\epsilon>0
small enough so that

\Big|\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-\frac{(1-E^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{E}\frac{(r_{+}(\ell)-r)(r-2M)}{2r^{\frac{1}{2}}(-a(\ell)-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r+\ell^{2})}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell_{0})-\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}M}\Big)=:d(\ell_{0}),

for all
(x,p)\in\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\}\cap\{|E(x,p)-E_{-}(\ell)|<{\epsilon}\}
. Next, we integrate as in (
115
) the derivative along the geodesic flow of
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
. As a result, the momentum coordinate
p^{r}
satisfies

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-d(\ell_{0})\Big)t(s)\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{t(s)}{8\sqrt{2}M}\Big),

where we have used (
118
) in the last estimate. Finally, we note that
\varphi_{+}^{\ell}
is bounded above in the domain
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}\cap\{|E-E_{-}(\ell)|<{\epsilon}\}
. Thus,

\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|=\Big|\varphi_{+}^{\ell}\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(t(s)),p(t(s))\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\Big(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ell)-d(\ell_{0})\Big)t(s)\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{t(s)}{8\sqrt{2}M}\Big),

where we have used again (
118
).
∎

7.
Decay in time for dispersive Vlasov fields on Schwarzschild

In this section, we obtain the decay estimates for the energy-momentum tensor
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
stated in Theorem
3.1
, Theorem
3.2
, and Theorem
3.3
. The proofs of the main results are based on the concentration estimates previously obtained on the stable manifolds associated unstable trapping, degenerate trapping at ISCO, and parabolic trapping at infinity. The estimates obtained in the previous section control the flow in a neighbourhood of the stable manifolds. In this section, we also estimate the flow in the rest of the mass-shell. We recall that the initial distribution function
f_{0}
is assumed to be compactly supported.

7.1.
A priori estimates in the near-horizon region

In this subsection, we show that we can assume without loss of generality that the initial distribution function
f_{0}
is supported away of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Lemma 7.1.1
.

There exists
r_{0}>2M
and
V_{0}>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<r_{0}\}
with
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})
, we have
v(s)\leq V_{0}
for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

By the compact support assumption of
f_{0}
in the momentum variables, there exist
r_{0}>2M
and an advanced time
V_{0}>0
, such that every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}
crosses
\mathcal{H}^{+}
in
\{v\leq V_{0}\}
for
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})\,\cap\,\{r\leq r_{0}\}
. This property holds because the momentum
p
is strictly away of the null generator of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
in
T_{x}\mathcal{E}
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})\,\cap\,\{r=2M\}
. We are using here the compact support of
f_{0}
.
∎

Remark 7.1.1
.

Lemma
7.1.1
only holds for massive Vlasov fields
f
for which the initial data
f_{0}
are compactly supported in the subset
\Sigma
of the mass-shell over the initial hypersurface.

Lemma 7.1.2
.

There exist constants
L_{1}\geq 0
, and
E_{1}\geq E_{0}>0
such that for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})\,\cap\,\{r\geq r_{0}\}
, we have
\ell(x,p)\in[0,L_{1}]
, and
E(x,p)\in[E_{0},E_{1}]
.

Proof.

By the compact support of the initial distribution function
f_{0}
, there exist
E_{1}>0
and
L_{1}>0
such that
E(x,p)\in[0,E_{1}]
and
\ell(x,p)\in[0,L_{1}]
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})
. By the mass-shell relation, the particle energy
E
satisfies

\forall(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0}),\qquad E^{2}(x,p)\geq\Big(1-\dfrac{2M}{r}\Big)\Big(1+\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\geq 1-\frac{2M}{r}\geq 1-\frac{2M}{r_{0}}=:E_{0}^{2}>0.

∎

Next, we upgrade Lemma
7.1.1
by removing the assumption that
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})
.

Corollary 7.1.3
.

There exist
r_{0}>2M
and
V_{1}>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<r_{0}\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
with particle energy
E\geq E_{0}
, we have
v(s)\leq V_{1}
for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.1.1
, we have
|\ell(x,p)|\leq L_{1}
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f)
. As a result, we have

(119)

4\Omega^{2}p^{u}p^{v}=\frac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}+1\leq\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{4M^{2}}+1.

Furthermore, the normalised momentum coordinate
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
satisfies

\Omega^{2}p^{u}=E-\Omega^{2}p^{v}\geq E_{0}-\Omega^{2}p^{v}\geq E_{0}-\Omega,

where we have used Lemma
7.1.2
in the first estimate, and (
40
) in the second one. By setting
r_{0}-2M
small enough, we obtain that
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
is strictly away from zero, so
p^{v}
can be bounded above by using (
119
). Thus, for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f)\,\cap\,\{r=2M\}
, the momentum
p
is strictly away from the null generator of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. We are using here the compact support assumption on
f_{0}
. From this, the result follows.
∎

In the rest of the section, we proceed to show the main results of the article

7.2.
Decay for Vlasov fields compactly supported on
\mathcal{D}_{0}

In this subsection, we prove time decay for the energy-momentum tensor
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
of a Vlasov field
f
with initial data compactly supported on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
. We note that there exist constants
E_{1}>E_{0}>1
and
L_{1}\geq 0
, such that
E(x,p)\in[E_{0},E_{1}]
and
\ell(x,p)\in[0,L_{1}]
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f_{0})
. This properties hold by the compact support assumption on the initial distribution function
f_{0}
.

The proof of the decay estimates of the energy-momentum tensor
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
follow by proving decay of the momentum support of the distribution
f
. In this subsection, we will show the decay of the momentum support by proving concentration estimates on the stable manifolds associated to the energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
for
\ell\geq 4M
. We obtain concentration estimates in terms of the coordinate
p^{v}
to apply these estimates later to the components of
\mathrm{T}_{\mu\nu}
.

7.2.1.
Estimates in the bounded region

First, we recall that the coordinate
p^{v}
can be written as

(120)

p^{v}=\frac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}(E\,+\,p^{r}).

In the following, we denote the coordinate
p^{v}
of past-trapped and future-trapped geodesics with angular momentum
\ell>2\sqrt{3}M
and particle energy
E=E_{-}(\ell)
, by
p^{v,+}_{\ell}
and
p^{v,-}_{\ell}
, respectively. By (
120
), we deduce

p^{v,\pm}_{\ell}(r):=\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(E_{-}(\ell)\,+\,p^{r,\pm}_{\ell}(r)\Big),\qquad\quad p^{r,\pm}_{\ell}(r)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\pm(r-r_{-}(\ell))\Big)\sqrt{E^{2}_{-}(\ell)\,-\,V_{\ell}(r)},

where we have used the mass-shell relation. We will now show a concentration estimate in terms of
p^{v}
using the bounds previously derived for
p^{r}
in Proposition
6.3.1
.

Lemma 7.2.1
.

There exists
R>2M
such that for every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
with angular momentum
\ell\geq 4M
and particle energy
E\geq E_{0}>1
, we have

(121)

|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v(s)\Big).

Proof.

By the monotonicity of the map
\ell\mapsto E_{-}(\ell)
, there exists
\ell_{0}>4M
such that
E_{0}=E_{-}(\ell_{0})
. As a result, unstable trapping only holds for
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
in
\supp(f)
. We assume without loss of generality that
f
is supported on
\{\ell\geq\ell_{0},\,|E-E_{-}(\ell)|\leq\frac{1}{2}(E_{0}-1)\}
, because of the compact support assumption on the initial data. The geodesics in the complement
\supp(f)\setminus(\{\ell\geq\ell_{0},\,|E-E_{-}(\ell)|\leq\frac{1}{2}(E_{0}-1)\})
either cross
\mathcal{H}^{+}
or leave the region
\{r<R\}
, before the advanced time
v
is sufficiently large.

The subregion
r\sim r_{-}(\ell)
.
We first prove the estimate (
121
) in the region
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}
. By Proposition
6.3.1
, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that for every geodesic
\gamma\colon[s_{1},s_{2}]\to\{r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
with angular momentum
\ell\geq 4M
and particle energy
E\in(E_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon,E_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}\Big(v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})\Big)\Big),

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}}\,-\,\frac{2M}{1-E^{2}_{-}(\ell)}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}\Big(v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})\Big)\Big),

for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f)\,\cap\,\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}
. Thus, the difference
E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)
satisfies that

|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|\lesssim|E^{2}\,-\,E^{2}_{-}(\ell)|=|(p^{r})^{2}\,-\,(p^{r,+}_{\ell})^{2}|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}\Big(v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})\Big)\Big).

where we have used that
E\,+\,E_{-}(\ell)\geq 1
for all
\ell\geq 4M
. Moreover, the difference
p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}
satisfies

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|+\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}\Big(v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})\Big)\Big).

Away from
r\sim r_{-}(\ell)
and
r\sim 2M
.
We consider next the complement of the domain
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|<\epsilon\}
as a subset of
\{r_{0}<r<R\}
. Let us bound the advanced time that geodesics spend in
\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon\}\,\cup\,\{r_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon<r<R\}
. There exists
C_{0}>0
such that

(122)

(p^{r})^{2}=E^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)>C_{0}^{2}>0,

in the region
\{r>r_{0}\}
, since the turning points for these orbits occur for
r\sim r_{-}(\ell)
. As a result, for every geodesic
\gamma
contained in
\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon\}\,\cup\,\{r_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon<r<R\}
, we have

(123)

\forall s_{2}\geq s_{1},\qquad\quad v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})\lesssim\int_{v(s_{1})}^{v(s_{2})}\Omega^{2}(r)\mathrm{d}v\leq\int_{v(s_{1})}^{v(s_{2})}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{p^{v}}=s_{2}-s_{1}=\int_{r(s_{1})}^{r(s_{2})}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{p^{r}}\lesssim R,

where we have used that
\Omega^{2}p^{v}\leq E\leq E_{1}
, and (
122
). We obtain that in finite advanced time every geodesic leaves
\{|r-r_{-}(\ell)|>\epsilon\}\,\cap\,\{r<R\}
.

By the contraction property of
\varphi_{-}^{\ell}
in (
69
), we have the monotonicity property

(124)

\forall\,s_{2}\geq s_{1},\qquad\quad\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(s_{2}),p(s_{2})\Big)\Big|\leq\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(s_{1}),p(s_{1})\Big)\Big|.

Let us consider a geodesic
\gamma\colon[s_{1},s_{2}]\to\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)-\epsilon\}\,\cup\,\{r_{-}(\ell)+\epsilon<r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
. By the monotonicity property of
\varphi_{-}
, and the uniform bound on the advanced time away from the trapped set, we have that

\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(s_{2}),p(s_{2})\Big)\Big|\leq\Big|\varphi_{-}^{\ell}\Big(x(s_{1}),p(s_{1})\Big)\Big|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v(s_{1})\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v(s_{2})\Big),

since
(x(s_{1}),p(s_{1}))
can be assumed to be in
\{r-r_{-}(\ell)\leq\epsilon\}
. Finally, we follow the same arguments used in the subregion
r\sim r_{-}(\ell)
to show that (
121
) holds. First, one propagates the estimates (
117
) in Proposition
6.3.1
. As a result, we control the difference
E^{2}\,-\,E^{2}_{-}(\ell)
, and then the difference
p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))
.

The subregion
r\sim 2M
.
We use first Proposition
4.4.2
to obtain that

\dfrac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r))(s_{2})=\dfrac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}+r^{2}}(p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r))(s_{1})\exp\Big(-\int_{v(s_{1})}^{v(s_{2})}\frac{2M}{(r^{2}+\ell^{2})r^{2}}\Big(r^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{M}r+3\ell^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\Big),

for any piece of geodesic. We remark that the exponent in the RHS of the identity above is negative for
r-2M
sufficiently small. Thus, we have the estimate

|p^{v}(s_{2})\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s_{2}))|\lesssim|p^{v}(s_{1})\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s_{1}))|\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v(s_{1})\Big)\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v(s_{2})\Big)

since the difference
v(s_{2})-v(s_{1})
is uniformly bounded.

The estimate (
121
) follows by putting together the estimates performed in the three regions considered. We note that the constant in the RHS of (
121
) is uniform among the geodesics under study.
∎

We show now the estimates in Theorem
3.1
in the bounded region of spacetime.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
in the bounded region
. By Lemma
7.2.1
, the support of the distribution function in the coordinate
p^{v}
decays exponentially. Then, for all
x\in\{r<R\}
, the component
{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f]
of the energy-momentum tensor satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}f(x,p)p_{u}p_{v}\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{4\Omega^{2}}\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}f(x,p)(\Omega^{2}p^{u})(\Omega^{2}p^{v})\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{\exp(\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v)}\int_{r^{4}(g_{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}})_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}\leq L_{1}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{\exp(\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}M}v)},

where we have estimated
\Omega^{2}p^{u}
using Lemma
4.5.4
. The same argument shows the decay estimates for the other components of
\mathrm{T}_{\mu\nu}
.

7.2.2.
Estimates in the far-away region

We begin proving a comparison lemma between the retarded time
u(s)
and the radial coordinate
r(s)
, along outgoing geodesics in the far-away region.

Lemma 7.2.2
.

There exists
R>2M
such that for every outgoing geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
with angular momentum
\ell\geq 4M
, and particle energy
E\geq E_{0}>1
, we have
u(s)\lesssim r(s)
for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

There exists
R>2M
such that every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
is outgoing, since
E>1
on the support of the distribution
f
. We can assume that
R>2M
is large enough so that

\forall(x,p)\in\{r>R\},\qquad p^{r}=\Big(E^{2}-1+\dfrac{2M}{r}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{2M\ell^{2}}{r^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\dfrac{\sqrt{E^{2}-1}}{2},

for every outgoing geodesic. We are using here that
E\geq E_{0}>1
. As a result, the parameter
s
along a geodesic
\gamma
in
\supp(f)
satisfies

(125)

s=\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{p^{r}}=\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{\sqrt{E^{2}-V_{\ell}(r)}}\lesssim r(s).

On the other hand, the coordinate
p^{u}
satisfies
\Omega^{2}p^{u}\leq E\leq E_{1}
, so we have

(126)

s=\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{p^{u}}\geq\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\Omega^{2}(r)\mathrm{d}u\gtrsim u(s),

for every geodesic
\gamma
in
\{r>R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
. Thus, we have
r(s)\gtrsim u(s)
for all
s\in[0,a]
.
∎

We conclude the proof of Theorem
3.1
with the estimates in the far-away region.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
in the far-away region
. By Proposition
4.5.3
, the support of the distribution in the coordinate
p^{v}
decays like
r^{-1}
. We are using here that
E>1
. Then, for all
x\in\{r>R\}
, the component
{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f]
of the energy-momentum tensor satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f]

\displaystyle=\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}f(x,p)p_{u}p_{v}\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}f(x,p)(\Omega^{2}p^{u})(\Omega^{2}p^{v})\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}\int_{r^{4}(g_{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}})_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}\leq L_{1}}r\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{r^{3}}\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{u^{3}}.

where we used (
125
)–(
126
) in the last estimate. The same argument shows the decay estimates for the other components of
\mathrm{T}_{\mu\nu}
.

7.3.
Decay for Vlasov fields supported up to the boundary of
\mathcal{D}_{1}

The proof of Theorem
3.2
follows by the same strategy followed in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. In the bounded region, the estimates can be directly extended to
f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
because the normal hyperbolicity of the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
holds for all
\ell\leq 4M
. Let us prove now the decay estimates in the far-away region. We note that there exist constants
E_{1}\geq 1
and
L_{1}\geq 0
, such that
E(x,p)\in[1,E_{1}]
and
\ell(x,p)\in[0,L_{0}]
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}})
. These a priori bounds hold because of the compact support assumption of
f_{0}
.

7.3.1.
Estimates in the far-away region

We first prove a comparison lemma between the retarded time
u(s)
and the radial coordinate
r(s)
along outgoing orbits.

Lemma 7.3.1
.

There exists
R>2M
such that for every outgoing geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
with angular momentum
\ell\geq 4M
, and particle energy
E\geq 1
, we have
u(s)\lesssim r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)
for all
s\in[0,a]
.

Proof.

There exists
R>2M
such that every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r>R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f)
is outgoing, since
E\geq 1
on the support of the distribution
f
. By the estimate (
38
) in the proof of Proposition
4.5.3
, the radial coordinate
r(s)
along outgoing geodesics with
E\geq 1
satisfies
p^{r}(s)\gtrsim r^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s)
. Thus, we have

(127)

r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)\geq\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}r\gtrsim\int_{r(0)}^{r(s)}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{p^{r}}=\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{p^{u}}\geq\int_{u(0)}^{u(s)}\Omega^{2}(r)\mathrm{d}u\gtrsim u(s),

where we used
\Omega^{2}p^{u}\leq E\leq E_{1}
. Thus, we have
r^{\frac{3}{2}}(s)\gtrsim u(s)
for all
s\in[0,a]
.
∎

We perform now the estimates in Theorem
3.2
in the far-away region.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
in the far-away region
. By Proposition
4.5.3
, the support of the distribution in the coordinate
p^{v}
decays like
r^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.We are using here that
E\geq 1
. Thus, for all
x\in\{r>R\}
, the component
{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}]
of the energy-momentum tensor satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathrm{T}}_{uv}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}]

\displaystyle=\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}})(x,p)p_{u}p_{v}\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{p^{v}}\int_{p^{A}}\int_{p^{B}}(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}})(x,p)(\Omega^{2}p^{u})(\Omega^{2}p^{v})\dfrac{r^{2}\sqrt{\det\gamma}}{p^{v}}\mathrm{d}p^{v}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{r^{4}(g_{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}})_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}\leq L_{1}}\mathrm{d}p^{A}\mathrm{d}p^{B}

(128)

\displaystyle\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\dfrac{\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,p}}}{u^{\frac{5}{3}}},

where we used (
127
) in the last estimate. The same argument shows the decay estimates for the other components of
\mathrm{T}_{\mu\nu}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}]
.

7.4.
Decay for Vlasov fields supported up to the boundary of
\mathcal{D}

Let us prove decay in time for the energy-momentum tensor
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]
of a Vlasov field
f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}
supported on
\mathcal{D}
. The proof of the decay estimates for the energy-momentum tensor follow by proving decay of the momentum support of the distribution. Note that there exist
E_{1}\geq 0
and
L_{1}\geq 0
, such that
E(x,p)\in[0,E_{1}]
and
\ell(x,p)\in[0,L_{1}]
for all
(x,p)\in\supp(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}})
.

We have previously obtained time decay for the energy-momentum tensor
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
of a Vlasov field
f
with initial data compactly supported on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. In order to use our previous decay estimates, we decompose the distribution
f
as

f=f_{\geq 1}+f_{<1}:=f\chi_{\{E\geq 1\}}+f\chi_{\{E<1\}},

where
\chi_{\{E\geq 1\}}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to[0,1]
and
\chi_{\{E<1\}}\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to[0,1]
are standard characteristic functions. As a result, the components
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]
of the energy-momentum tensor of the Vlasov field
f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}
satisfy

{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]={\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\geq 1}\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]+{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{<1}\chi_{\mathcal{D}}],

for every
\mu
,
\nu
, where
\chi_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is the characteristic function of
\mathcal{D}
. We will show that
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\geq 1}\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]
decays faster than
{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{<1}\chi_{\mathcal{D}}]
, in both, the bounded and the far-away regions.

Let us now focus on the region
\{E<1\}
. We will analyse the momentum support of the distribution function in the following four subregions of phase space:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{a}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)\in\Big(2\sqrt{3}M,4M\Big),\,E(x,p)\in\Big(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30}\Big)\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{b}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)\in(2\sqrt{2}M,2\sqrt{3}M),\,E(x,p)\in\Big(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30}\Big)\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{c}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)\in(0,2\sqrt{3}M),\,E(x,p)\in\Big(\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30},1\Big)\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{d}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(x,p)\in{\mathcal{P}}:\ell(x,p)\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M),E(x,p)\in\Big(\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30},1\Big)\Big\}.

In the complementary region of the domain
\mathcal{D}
, given by

\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{e}}:=\Big\{(x,p):E(x,p)<1\Big\}\setminus\Big(\mathcal{D}_{a}\,\cup\,\mathcal{D}_{b}\,\cup\,\mathcal{D}_{c}\,\cup\,\mathcal{D}_{d}\Big),

geodesics cross
\mathcal{H}^{+}
before the advanced time
v
is sufficiently large.

In the bounded region, we assume without loss of generality that the Vlasov field
f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}
is supported in the regions
\mathcal{D}_{a}
,
\mathcal{D}_{b}
,
\mathcal{D}_{c}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{d}
. In the far-away region, we assume instead that the Vlasov field
f\chi_{\mathcal{D}}
is supported in the regions
\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{c}}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{\textrm{d}}
. The regions
\mathcal{D}_{a}
,
\mathcal{D}_{b}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{e}
, only concern the decay estimates in the bounded region.

7.4.1.
Estimates in the bounded region

We first show concentration estimates in a neighbourhood of the sphere
\{r=6M\}
of trapped orbits. We recall that the hyperbolicity of the radial flow on the spheres of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
degenerates when
\ell=2\sqrt{3}M
. We will show inverse polynomial decay estimates in this regime. We begin considering the case when
\ell\leq 2\sqrt{3}M
.

Lemma 7.4.1
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}})
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{2}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

(129)

\displaystyle\Big|p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{E}{2\Omega^{2}(s)}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\geq r_{-}(\ell),

(130)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\leq r_{-}(\ell).

Proof.

In this region of phase space, trapping only holds at
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)\subset\{r=6M\}
. We also recall that the stable manifolds
W^{\pm}(2\sqrt{3}M)
associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\sqrt{3}M)
, are contained in
\{r\leq 6M\}
. As a result, we can assume without loss of generality that the geodesics considered in this lemma are contained in
\{r\leq 7M\}
. We also assume without loss of generality that
\ell\geq\ell_{0}>\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M
for some
\ell_{0}>\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M
. We make this assumption to avoid any discussion for
\ell\sim\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M
where
E_{-}(\ell)
is not defined anymore. See Proposition
6.1.4
for more details.

Let
r_{0}>2M
such that
r_{0}-2M
is small enough. Let
\ell\in(\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
. We divide the analysis in four different subregions: when
r_{-}(\ell)\leq r\leq 7M
, when
5M\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)
, when
2M+r_{0}\leq r\leq 5M
, and when
r\leq r_{0}
. We address these subregions in the same order.

The subregion
r_{-}(\ell)\leq r\leq 7M
. By Proposition
6.1.7
, for every geodesic in
\{5M\leq r\leq 7M\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\geq r_{-}(\ell).

Thus, the difference
p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-2}E
satisfies that

\displaystyle\Big|p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{E}{2\Omega^{2}(s)}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we used the relation
p^{v}=\frac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}(E+p^{r}).

The subregion
5M\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)
. By Proposition
6.1.7
, for every geodesic in
\{5M\leq r\leq 7M\}
with angular momentum
\ell\in(\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}M,2\sqrt{3}M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|=\Big|\frac{p^{r}}{\sqrt{1-E^{2}}}\,-\,\frac{(r_{-}-r)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big((r-r_{-})^{2}+\frac{r_{-}^{3}}{r_{-}^{2}-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}r_{-}+\ell^{2}}\Big(\frac{\ell^{2}}{2M}-r_{-}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\lesssim v^{-3}(s).

Thus, the difference
p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}
satisfies that

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|+\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)-p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used the estimate for the particle energy in Proposition
6.1.7
.

The subregion
r_{0}\leq r\leq 5M
.
The analysis in this subregion follows by the same arguments performed at the end of the proof of Lemma
7.2.1
. We simply propagate to this region the concentration estimate obtained in the subregion
\{5M\leq r\leq 7M\}
.

The subregion
r\leq r_{0}
.
The analysis in this subregion follows by the same arguments performed at the end of the proof of Lemma
7.2.1
. Specifically, we use the red-shift effect in the form presented in Proposition
4.4.2
.

The estimates (
129
)–(
130
) follow by putting together the estimates performed in the three subregions considered above.
∎

We will now consider the case of orbits with angular momentum
\ell\geq 2\sqrt{3}M
. In the next lemma, we estimate the geodesic flow in a neighbourhood of the homoclinic orbits at the energy level
\{E=E_{-}(\ell)\}
when
\ell
is strictly smaller than
4M
.

Let us recall that there exists a uniform constant
A>2M
such that
-a(\ell)\leq A
for every geodesic with
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
. This property was obtained in the proof of Proposition
6.2.5
.

Lemma 7.4.2
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}})
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

(131)

\displaystyle\Big|p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{E}{2\Omega^{2}(r(s))}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r\in[-a(\ell),R+A],

(132)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad r\in[r_{-}(\ell),-a(\ell)],

(133)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,-}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0\quad\text{and}\quad r\in[r_{-}(\ell),-a(\ell)],

(134)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r\in[2M,r_{-}(\ell)].

Proof.

In this region of phase space, unstable trapping only holds for
\ell\leq\ell_{1}
for some
\ell_{1}<4M
. The homoclinic orbits associated to the sphere of trapped orbits
\mathcal{S}^{-}(\ell)
, are contained in the region
\{r\leq A\}
for
A>2M
. Thus, we can assume without loss of generality that the geodesics considered in this lemma are contained in
\{r\leq A\}
.

Let
r_{0}>2M
such that
r_{0}-2M
is small enough. Let
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,\ell_{1})
. We divide the analysis in five different subregions: when
-a(\ell)\leq r\leq R+A
, when
r_{-}(\ell)\leq r\leq-a(\ell)
, when
5M\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)
, when
r_{0}\leq r\leq 5M
, and when
r\leq r_{0}
. We address these subregions in the same order.

The subregion
-a(\ell)\leq r\leq R+A
. By Proposition
6.2.2
, for every geodesic in
-a(\ell)\leq r\leq R+A
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad r(s)\geq-a(\ell).

Thus, the difference
p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-2}E
satisfies that

\displaystyle\Big|p^{v}(s)\,-\,\frac{E}{2\Omega^{2}(s)}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{3}(s)},

where we used that
p^{v}=\frac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}(E+p^{r}).

The subregion
r_{-}(\ell)\leq r\leq-a(\ell)
. By Proposition
6.2.2
, for every geodesic in
r_{-}(\ell)\leq r\leq-a(\ell)
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0,

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,-}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{3}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0.

Thus, the differences
p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}
and
p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,-}_{\ell}
satisfy that

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|+\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\geq 0,

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,-}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|+\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,-}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}\leq 0,

where we have used the estimate for the particle energy in Proposition
6.2.2
.

The subregion
5M\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)
. By Proposition
6.2.2
, for every geodesic in
5M\leq r\leq r_{-}(\ell)
with angular momentum
\ell\in(2\sqrt{3}M,4M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{2}{3},\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30})
, we have

\displaystyle|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\lesssim v^{-3}(s).

Thus, the difference
p^{v}\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}
satisfies that

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|E\,-\,E_{-}(\ell)|+\dfrac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}|p^{r}(s)\,-\,p^{r,+}_{\ell}(r(s))|\lesssim v^{-3}(s),

where we have used the estimate for the particle energy in Proposition
6.2.2
.

The regions
r_{0}\leq r\leq 5M
and
r\leq r_{0}
can be taken care in the same fashion as in the proof of Proposition
7.4.1
. The estimates (
131
)–(
133
) follow by putting together the estimates performed in the five subregions considered above.
∎

Next, we will show concentration estimates in a neighbourhood of the parabolic manifolds at infinity. The leading contribution in the energy-momentum tensor in the bounded region comes from this region. Let us recall again that the coordinate
p^{v}
can be written as

(135)

p^{v}=\frac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}(E\,+\,p^{r}).

We denote the coordinate
p^{v}
of past-trapped and future-trapped geodesics with angular momentum
\ell
and particle energy
E=1
, by
p^{v,+}_{\ell,1}
and
p^{v,-}_{\ell,1}
, respectively. By (
135
), we deduce

p^{v,\pm}_{\ell,1}(r)=\frac{1}{2\Omega^{2}}\Big(E\,+\,p^{r,\pm}_{\ell,1}(r)\Big),\quad\qquad\quad p^{r,\pm}_{\ell,1}(r)=\mp\sqrt{1\,-\,V_{\ell}(r)},

where we have used the mass-shell relation. We will now show a concentration estimate for
p^{v}
using the bounds for
p^{r}
in Propositions
6.1.2
and
6.2.1
. We begin considering the case when
\ell\leq 2\sqrt{3}M
.

Lemma 7.4.3
.

For every geodesic
\gamma_{x,p}\colon[0,a]\to\{r<R\}\,\cap\,\supp(f\chi_{\mathcal{D}})
with angular momentum
\ell\in(0,2\sqrt{3}M)
and particle energy
E\in(\frac{21\sqrt{2}}{30},1)
, we have

(136)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,-}_{\ell,1}(r(s))|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}(s)\geq 0,

(137)

\displaystyle|p^{v}(s)\,-\,p^{v,+}_{\ell,1}(r(s))|\lesssim\dfrac{1}{v^{\frac{1}{3}}(s)},\qquad\text{if}\quad p^{r}(s)\leq 0.

Proof.

We can assume with out loss of generality that every geodesic
\gamma
is incoming in the bounded region
\{r<R\}
, by the compact support assumption on the initial data. Let
r_{0}>2M
such that
r_{0}-2M
is small enough. We divide the analysis in three different subregions: when
r\geq 16M
, when
r_{0}\leq r\leq 16M
, and when
r\leq r_{0}
. We address these subregions in the same order.

The subregion
r\geq 16M
. By Proposition
6.1.2
